\displaystyle=\theta(\omega_{b})\SumInt_{X_{\bar{n}}}\Bigl\langle 0\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{\bar{n}}^{\beta^{\prime}\bar{b}^{\prime}}(b_{\perp})\Bigr\rvert h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigl\langle h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rvert g{\mathcal{B}}_{\bar{n}\perp,\,\xi\omega_{b}}^{\rho\,b\bar{c}}(0)\,\chi_{\bar{n},\,-(1-\xi)\omega_{b}}^{\beta c}(0)\Bigr\rvert 0\Bigr\rangle\,.

(4.252)

In both cases,
\rho
is a Lorentz index, while
\beta\beta^{\prime}
are Dirac indices,
and
\xi
is the momentum fraction of the total momentum
\omega_{a,b}

that is carried away by the collinear gluon. Note that the gluon momenta have different signs,
as in
\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}
the gluon is incoming (
-\xi\omega_{a}<0
), while in
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}

the gluon is outgoing (
\xi\omega_{b}>0
). The color index
c
is summed over,
while
\bar{b}^{\prime}
and
b
are held fixed.
Note that these correlators differ from the quark-gluon-quark correlators
defined in ref.
[
21
]
, where only the total momentum the quark-gluon
fields that appear in the same collinear direction is constrained, such that there is no analog of our splitting variable
\xi
there.
We will discuss this further in
section
5.4.1
.

Similar to LP, the collinear matrix elements are diagonal in color,
allowing us to define color-traced objects analogous to eq. (
4.2
),

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho\beta\beta^{\prime}}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\theta(\omega_{a})\Bigl\langle N\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{n}^{\beta^{\prime}}(b_{\perp})\,g{\mathcal{B}}_{n\perp,\,-\xi\omega_{a}}^{\rho}(0)\,\chi_{n,\,(1-\xi)\omega_{a}}^{\beta}(0)\Bigr\rvert N\Bigr\rangle\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/\bar{f}}^{\dagger\,\rho\beta\beta^{\prime}}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{2zN_{c}}\delta_{\bar{b}b^{\prime}}\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/\bar{f}}^{\dagger\,\rho\,\beta\beta^{\prime}\,\bar{b}^{\prime}b}(\omega_{b},\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{2zN_{c}}\theta(\omega_{b})\SumInt_{X_{\bar{n}}}\operatorname{tr}\Bigl\langle 0\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{\bar{n}}^{\beta^{\prime}}(b_{\perp})\Bigr\rvert h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigl\langle h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rvert g{\mathcal{B}}_{\bar{n}\perp,\,\xi\omega_{b}}^{\rho}(0)\,\chi_{\bar{n},\,-(1-\xi)\omega_{b}}^{\beta}(0)\Bigr\rvert 0\Bigr\rangle\,.

(4.261)

Here,
x=\omega_{a}/P_{N}^{-}
and
z=P_{h}^{+}/\omega_{b}
are the same collinear momentum fractions as at LP.
We include a minus sign in
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\bar{f}}
to remove the relative sign between the two terms in eq. (
4.7
).
Applying eq. (
4.7
) together with eq. (
4.2
) to eq. (
4.7
), we obtain

\displaystyle W^{(1)\,\mu\nu}_{J^{(0)}J^{(1)}_{\mathcal{B}1}}

\displaystyle=-4z(\gamma_{\perp}^{\mu})^{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}(\gamma_{\perp\rho})^{\alpha\beta}\,\sum_{f}\int\!\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{b}}_{T}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\,e^{-\mathrm{i}\vec{q}_{T}\cdot\vec{b}_{T}}\int\!\mathrm{d}\xi\,\mathcal{H}_{f}^{(1)}(-\omega_{a}\omega_{b},\xi)

\displaystyle\quad\times\biggl[\frac{\omega_{a}n^{\nu}+\omega_{b}\bar{n}^{\nu}}{\omega_{b}}\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho\beta\beta^{\prime}}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})\,\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{h/f}^{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})\,S(b_{T})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\omega_{a}n^{\nu}+\omega_{b}\bar{n}^{\nu}}{\omega_{a}}\hat{B}_{\bar{f}/N}^{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/\bar{f}}^{\dagger\,\rho\beta\beta^{\prime}}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})\,S(b_{T})\biggr]\,.

(4.262)

We also defined the new hard function

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{f}^{(1)}(\tilde{q}^{2},\xi)=C_{f}^{(0)}(\tilde{q}^{2})C_{f}^{(1)}(\tilde{q}^{2},\xi)\,,

(4.263)

where as usual in SIDIS kinematics
\tilde{q}^{2}=-\omega_{a}\omega_{b}<0
.
To simplify our result, in the following we employ that in the factorization frame
we have
\omega_{a}=q^{+}=Q
and
\omega_{b}=-q^{-}=Q-q_{T}^{2}/Q
, see eq. (
2.48
),
and neglet the power suppressed
q_{T}^{2}
correction. With this choice, the factors
of
\omega_{a,b}
cancel in the square brackets in eq. (
4.7
).

We next consider the contribution from
J^{(0)\mu\dagger}J^{(1)\nu}_{\mathcal{B}2}
.
From eq. (
4.77
), we see that compared to the
J_{\mathcal{B}1}
current, the gluon
is now collinear to the opposite quark. This leads to the following new color-traced correlators:

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,\bar{f}/N}^{\dagger\,\rho\,\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=-\theta(\omega_{a})\operatorname{tr}\Bigl\langle N\Bigr\rvert\chi_{n}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(b_{\perp})\,\bar{\chi}_{n,\,(1-\xi)\omega_{a}}^{\alpha}(0)\,g{\mathcal{B}}_{n\perp,\,-\xi\omega_{a}}^{\rho}(0)\Bigr\rvert N\Bigr\rangle\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}^{\rho\,\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2zN_{c}}\theta(\omega_{b})\SumInt_{X_{\bar{n}}}\operatorname{tr}\Bigl\langle 0\Bigr\rvert\chi_{\bar{n}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(b_{\perp})\Bigr\rvert h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigl\langle h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{\bar{n},\,-(1-\xi)\omega_{b}}^{\alpha}(0)\,g{\mathcal{B}}_{\bar{n}\perp,\,\xi\omega_{b}}^{\rho}(0)\Bigr\rvert 0\Bigr\rangle\,.

(4.272)

Note in eqs. (
4.7
) and (
4.7
), we have written
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/\bar{f}}
and

\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,\bar{f}/N}
as daggered objects, defined as

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,\bar{f}/N}^{\dagger\,\rho\,\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\bigl[\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,\bar{f}/N}^{\rho\,\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}(x,\xi,-{\vec{b}}_{T})\bigr]^{\dagger}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/\bar{f}}^{\dagger\,\rho\,\beta\beta^{\prime}}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\bigl[\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/\bar{f}}^{\rho\beta\beta^{\prime}}(z,\xi,-{\vec{b}}_{T})\bigr]^{\dagger}\,.

(4.273)

The sign in
-{\vec{b}}_{T}
arises because by Lorentz invariance the correlator
only depends on the difference of the field positions, which under hermitian conjugation
flips sign,
[\chi_{n}(b_{\perp})\bar{\chi}_{n}(0)]^{\dagger}=\bar{\chi}_{n}(0)\chi_{n}(b_{\perp})
.

In our analysis here we are working in a general covariant gauge where transverse Wilson lines at light-cone infinity can be ignored. To obtain full gauge invariant results these lines must be included, and we do so for the quark-gluon-quark TMD PDF and TMD FF correlators in
appendix
C
. This results in the definitions in eqs. (
4.7
) and (
4.7
) being generalized in the manner given in eq. (
C
).

Since the
\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}
operators do not induce new soft Wilson lines,
as discussed in more detail in
section
4.1.4
, the factorized hadronic tensor
contains the LP soft function, which can be absorbed into these correlators
in the standard fashion,

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho\,\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho\,\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})\sqrt{S(b_{T})}\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}^{\rho\,\beta^{\prime}\beta}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}^{\rho\,\beta^{\prime}\beta}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})\sqrt{S(b_{T})}\,,

(4.274)

and likewise for the antiquark correlators. Here and in the following,
we will denote the soft-subtracted quark-gluon-quark correlators with a tilde.
Once again we remark that in SCET the procedure for carrying out soft subtractions at subleading power is well defined, and is contained in the matrix elements of collinear fields in eqs. (
4.7
) and (
4.7
), see
[
103
]
.

Also taking the complex conjugate
contributions into account, we arrive at our final factorized expression for the
\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}^{\mu}
contributions at NLP

\displaystyle W_{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=-4z\sum_{f}\int\!\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{b}}_{T}\,e^{-\mathrm{i}{\vec{q}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{b}}_{T}}\int\mathrm{d}\xi\,\mathcal{H}^{(1)}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-},\xi)\,(n^{\nu}+{\bar{n}}^{\nu})

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathrm{Tr}\left[\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})\gamma_{\perp}^{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{h/f}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})\gamma_{\perp\rho}+B_{f/N}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})\gamma_{\perp}^{\mu}\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}^{\rho}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})\gamma_{\perp\rho}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+(f\to\bar{f},\,\mu\leftrightarrow\nu,\,\mathcal{H}^{(1)}_{f}\to\mathcal{H}^{(1)*}_{f})+\text{h.c.}\,.

(4.275)

In eq. (
4.7
), h.c. includes exchanging
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu
as well as
flipping
{\vec{b}}_{T}\to-{\vec{b}}_{T}
in the correlators.
Since the
\mu\nu
indices involve one transverse index and the other one proportional to
n
or
{\bar{n}}
, the situation here is the same as for eq. (
4.229
): when contracting with the leading power projectors,
P_{i\mu\nu}^{(0)}
(given by eq. (
2.2.1
) with
\tilde{n}_{x,y,t}\to n_{x,y,t}
), eq. (
4.7
) has contributions to
W_{1,2,5,6}^{X}
. It does not contribute to
W_{-1,3,4,7}^{X}
at NLP, because their corresponding projectors have two transverse indices.
Note that eq. (
4.7
) provides a bare factorization theorem for the
qgq
contributions. While we do not consider its renormalization here, the terms resulting from renormalization are anticipated to have a similar form.

We can also provide evidence that the convolution over
\xi
in eq. (
4.7
) will be convergent to all orders in perturbation theory. This follows from the fact that in SCET divergent convolutions from a collinear momentum-fraction
\xi\to 0
are in one-to-one correspondence with the presence of a non-trivial soft contributions
[
103
]
. These soft contributions correctly describe the small
\xi\sim 0
momentum region, and the divergent convolution arises from not properly defining the separation between the collinear and soft contributions in the renormalized factorization theorem. (For an SCET example where such endpoint divergences have been treated, see Refs.
[
125
,
126
]
.) Since our analysis implies that there are no non-trivial subleading power soft contributions for SIDIS at NLP, we anticipate that the integrals over
\xi
will correspondingly all be converge.

5
Results

In this section, we explicitly calculate the factorization formulae for different structure functions. To this end, we calculate the spinor traces in the factorized hadronic tensor with substitutions of the spinor decompositions of the several correlators defined in the previous section. Then we contract the hadronic tensor with projectors to give rise to our final formulae of factorized structure functions.

We first show the leading power results in
section
5.1
, which are in full agreement with literature (e.g. ref.
[
21
]
). The analysis of
section
4
demonstrates that many potential contributions are absent at NLP, including in particular subleading soft corrections and contributions from time-ordered products with subleading power Lagrangian insertions. In
section
5.2
, we include the three non-zero NLP contributions (kinematic,
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
, and
\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}
) to derive factorization for the polarized and unpolarized structure functions
W_{1,2,5,6}^{X}
which start at NLP. We then discuss what is new in our results in
section
5.3
, and give an extensive comparison with previous literature in
section
5.4
.

5.1
Leading Power

To decompose our hadronic matrix elements
B_{f/N}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})
and
\mathcal{G}_{h/f}(z,{\vec{p}}_{T})

into independent structures, we mostly follow the conventions in ref.
[
21
]
.
There, the correlators are decomposed in momentum space, so we first require the Fourier-transformed correlators

\displaystyle B_{f/N}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{b}}_{T}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{+\mathrm{i}{\vec{b}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{k}}_{T}}B_{f/N}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})\,,\qquad\mathcal{G}_{h/f}(z,{\vec{p}}_{T})=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{b}}_{T}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-\mathrm{i}{\vec{b}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}\mathcal{G}_{h/f}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})\,.

(5.1)

Note that with some abuse of notation, we use the same symbols
B_{f/N}
and
\mathcal{G}_{h/f}
for Fourier and momentum space,
as the distinction between the two is clear from the arguments.
The different conventions for the Fourier signs can be understood as follows:
inserting the inverse eq. (
5.1
) into the LP hadronic tensor in eq. (
4.2
) yields

\displaystyle W^{(0)\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\sim H(q^{2})\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{b}}_{T}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-\mathrm{i}{\vec{b}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{q}}_{T}}B(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})\mathcal{G}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=H(q^{2})\int\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{k}}_{T}\,\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{p}}_{T}\,\delta^{(2)}({\vec{q}}_{T}+{\vec{k}}_{T}-{\vec{p}}_{T})B(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})\mathcal{G}(z,{\vec{p}}_{T})\,.

(5.2)

Since
q
and
N
are incoming, the sum of their transverse momenta must equal
that of the outgoing hadron, as also manifest in eq. (
4.103
).

Following the decomposition of the quark-quark correlators in ref.
[
21
]
, we write

\displaystyle B_{f/N}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\biggl[f_{1}-f_{1T}^{\perp}\frac{\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}k_{\perp\rho}S_{\perp\sigma}}{M_{N}}+g_{1L}\,S_{L}\gamma_{5}-g_{1T}\frac{k_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}}{M_{N}}\gamma_{5}+h_{1}\,\gamma_{5}\not{S}_{\perp}

\displaystyle\qquad+h_{1L}^{\perp}\,S_{L}\frac{\gamma_{5}\,\not{k}_{\perp}}{M_{N}}-h_{1T}^{\perp}\,\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{M_{N}^{2}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2}g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{\rho}k_{\perp}^{\sigma}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\Bigr)S_{\perp\,\rho}\gamma_{\sigma}\gamma_{5}+\mathrm{i}h_{1}^{\perp}\frac{\not{k}_{\perp}}{M_{N}}\biggr]\frac{\not{n}}{4}\,,

(5.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{h/f}(z,{\vec{p}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\Bigl(D_{1}+\mathrm{i}H_{1}^{\perp}\frac{\not{p}_{\perp}}{M_{h}}\Bigr)\frac{\not{{\bar{n}}}}{4}\,.

(5.4)

For more details on how to relate our definitions to those in ref.
[
21
]

and obtain this decomposition from theirs, see
appendix
D
.
Note that our correlators use SCET and hence are defined in terms of good fermion field components only.
Therefore only leading-twist terms contribute to eqs. (
5.1
) and (
5.4
).
For the fragmentation function we do not give polarization-dependent pieces, as we only consider the target hadron to be polarized.
On the right-hand side of both eqs. (
5.1
) and (
5.4
), we keep the quark flavor
f
implicit for each individual TMD PDF and TMD FF and suppress their arguments for brevity (which also include
\mu
and the Collins-Soper scales
\zeta_{a,b})
.
Finally, we remark that the decompositions in eqs. (
5.1
) and (
5.4
)
apply to the soft-subtracted TMDs, as defined in eq. (
4.190
), while this soft
factor was not taken into account ref.
[
21
]
.

For the antiquark distributions, one obtains similar decompositions as in eqs. (
5.1
) and (
5.4
)
upon taking into account that by charge conjugation
[
17
]

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[\gamma^{\mu}B_{\bar{f}/N}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[\gamma^{\mu}B_{f/N}^{c}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})\bigr]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}B_{\bar{f}/N}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})\bigr]

\displaystyle=-\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[\gamma^{\mu}\gamma_{5}B_{f/N}^{c}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})\bigr]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[\mathrm{i}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}B_{\bar{f}/N}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[\mathrm{i}\sigma^{\mu\nu}\gamma_{5}B_{f/N}^{c}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})\bigr]\,.

(5.5)

Here,
B^{c}_{f/N}
is the quark distribution evaluated with charge-conjugated fields
\chi^{c}=C\bar{\chi}^{T}
.
Eq. (
5.1
) implies that the distributions corresponding to unpolarized and transversely polarized quarks
have identical decompositions for quarks and antiquarks, while those for longitudinally polarized quarks receive a sign flip.
In our case, this only affects
g_{1L}
and
g_{1T}
.
For the spin-averaged TMDFF, it follows that the we have identical decomposition for quarks and antiquarks.

Using eqs. (
B
) and (
B.10
), we obtain the Fourier transform of eqs. (
5.1
) and (
5.4
),

\displaystyle B_{f/N}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\biggl[f_{1}+\mathrm{i}f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}M_{N}b_{\perp\,\rho}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}S_{\perp\sigma}+g_{1L}\,S_{L}\gamma_{5}+\mathrm{i}g_{1T}^{(1)}M_{N}b_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}\gamma_{5}+h_{1}\,\gamma_{5}\not{S}_{\perp}

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathrm{i}h_{1L}^{\perp(1)}\,S_{L}M_{N}\gamma_{5}{\not{b}}_{\perp}+h_{1}^{\perp(1)}M_{N}\not{b}_{\perp}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}h_{1T}^{\perp(2)}M_{N}^{2}b_{\perp}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{2}g^{\rho\sigma}_{\perp}-\frac{b_{\perp}^{\rho}b_{\perp}^{\sigma}}{b_{\perp}^{2}}\right)S_{\perp\,\rho}\gamma_{\sigma}\gamma_{5}\biggr]\frac{\not{n}}{4}\,,

(5.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{h/f}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\Bigl(D_{1}-H_{1}^{\perp(1)}M_{h}\not{b}_{\perp}\Bigr)\frac{\not{{\bar{n}}}}{4}\,.

(5.7)

Here, we used the abbreviations
[
127
]
19
19

19

Ref.
[
127
]
includes factors of
z
for the Fourier transform of the TMDFF
due to defining it with respect to the hadron momentum
\vec{P}_{T,h}
in a frame where the fragmenting
quark has no transverse momentum, while we work in a frame with vanishing
\vec{P}_{T,h}
and thus
the quark has nonvanishing transverse momentum
{\vec{p}}_{T}
.

\displaystyle f(x,b_{T})

\displaystyle=2\pi\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}p_{T}\,p_{T}J_{0}(b_{T}p_{T})f(x,p_{T})\,,

\displaystyle f^{(n)}(b_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi\,n!}{(b_{T}M)^{n}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}p_{T}\,p_{T}\left(\frac{p_{T}}{M}\right)^{n}J_{n}(b_{T}p_{T})\,f(p_{T})\,.

(5.8)

We next calculate the Dirac trace part required in eq. (
4.2
),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[B_{f/N}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})\gamma_{\perp}^{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{h/f}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})\gamma_{\perp}^{\nu}\bigr]

(5.9)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\biggl\{-\Bigl[f_{1}+\mathrm{i}f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}\bigl(M_{N}b_{\perp\rho}S_{\perp\sigma}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\bigr)\Bigr]D_{1}\,g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}+\Bigl[\mathrm{i}g_{1L}\,S_{L}-M_{N}\,b_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}\,g_{1T}^{(1)}\Bigr]D_{1}\,\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\quad+2h_{1}^{\perp(1)}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}M_{N}M_{h}b_{\perp}^{2}\,\left(\frac{1}{2}g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}-\frac{b_{\perp}^{\mu}b_{\perp}^{\nu}}{b_{\perp}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\mathrm{i}h_{1}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}M_{h}\left(b_{\perp}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}S_{\perp\rho}+S_{\perp}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+h^{\perp(1)}_{1L}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\,M_{N}M_{h}S_{L}\,\left(b_{\perp}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}+b_{\perp}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}h_{1T}^{\perp(2)}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}M_{N}^{2}M_{h}\Bigl[\frac{1}{2}b_{\perp}^{2}\bigl(b_{\perp}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}S_{\perp\rho}+S_{\perp}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\bigr)-(b_{\perp}{\cdot}S_{\perp})\left(b_{\perp}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}+b_{\perp}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\right)\Bigr]\biggr\}.

To obtain the structure functions, we first insert the trace in eq. (
5.9
)
into the LP hadronic tensor, eq. (
4.2
), and contract the result with the LP projectors
P_{i\mu\nu}^{(0)}
obtained from eq. (
2.2.1
) by taking
\tilde{n}_{x,y,t}\to n_{x,y,t}
.
By collecting the resulting expression with respect to
S_{L}
and
S_{T}
as given in eq. (
2.2.3
),
one then obtains the polarized structure functions defined in eq. (
2.2.3
).
The structure functions as classified by the angular coefficients are then
constructed following the middle column of eq. (
2.37
).
To express the resulting inverse Fourier transform in a compact fashion, we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{H}\,g^{(n)}\,D^{(m)}]

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-})\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{b}}_{T}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-\mathrm{i}{\vec{q}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{b}}_{T}}(-\mathrm{i}M_{N}b_{T})^{n}(\mathrm{i}M_{h}b_{T})^{m}\cos[(n+m)\varphi]

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\,g_{f}^{(n)}(x,b_{T})\,D_{f}^{(m)}(z,b_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-})\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}b_{T}\,b_{T}}{2\pi}(M_{N}b_{T})^{n}(-M_{h}b_{T})^{m}J_{n+m}(b_{T}q_{T})\,

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\,g_{f}^{(n)}(x,b_{T})\,D_{f}^{(m)}(z,b_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})\,.

(5.10)

Here,
\mathcal{H}
is the hard function, and
g^{(n)}
and
D^{(m)}
denote the Fourier-transformed
TMDPDF and TMDFF as defined in eq. (
5.1
), respectively. For brevity, we suppress
their arguments the sum over flavors on the left hand side, and they given explicitly on the right-hand side.
We have parameterized
{\vec{b}}_{T}=b_{T}(\cos\varphi,\sin\varphi)
,
such that with our convention for
{\vec{q}}_{T}=-q_{T}(1,0)
, we have
{\vec{q}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{b}}_{T}=-q_{T}b_{T}\cos\varphi
.
In the second step in eq. (
5.1
), we analytically evaluated the integral over
\varphi
.
We have also absorbed the common factors
(-\mathrm{i}M_{N}b_{T})
and
(+\mathrm{i}M_{h}b_{T})

that arise in the Fourier transforms of individual TMDs, compare eqs. (
5.1
) and (
5.1
).
The nonvanishing LP structure functions then read

\displaystyle W_{UU,T}

\displaystyle=\phantom{-}\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,f_{1}D_{1}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{UU}^{\cos 2\phi_{h}}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,h_{1}^{\perp(1)}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{UL}^{\sin 2\phi_{h}}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,h_{1L}^{\perp(1)}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{LL}

\displaystyle=\phantom{-}\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,g_{1L}D_{1}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{UT,T}^{\sin\left(\phi_{h}-\phi_{S}\right)}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}D_{1}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{UT}^{\sin\left(\phi_{h}+\phi_{S}\right)}

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,h_{1}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{UT}^{\sin\left(3\phi_{h}-\phi_{S}\right)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,h_{1T}^{\perp(2)}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{LT}^{\cos\left(\phi_{h}-\phi_{S}\right)}

\displaystyle=\phantom{-}\mathcal{F}\left[\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,g_{1T}^{(1)}D_{1}\right]\,.

(5.11)

Here, we suppress that these functions receive corrections in
\lambda
themselves,
which we will explicitly give in
section
5.2
.
These results agree with Eqs. (2.23) – (2.30) in ref.
[
127
]
,
up to a minus sign for all structures involving
H_{1}^{\perp\,(1)}
due
to the different sign in the definition of the Fourier transform for TMDFFs.

Note that in eq. (
5.1
), the structure functions
W_{LL}
and
W_{LT}^{\cos(\phi_{h}-\phi_{S})}

arise from the antisymmetric projector
P_{4}^{\mu\nu}
, where exchanging
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

for the antiquark contribution in
W^{\mu\nu}
yields a relative minus sign. Since corresponding
antiquark distributions
g_{1L}
and
g_{1T}^{(1)}
receive a minus sign on their own,
such that summing over all quarks and antiquarks as stated in eq. (
5.1
) is correct.
See also the comments below eq. (
4.2
) and eq. (
5.1
).

Result in Momentum Space

Traditionally, the above structure functions were expressed in momentum space
using convolutions of the form

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{F}}[\omega\,\mathcal{H}\,g\,D]

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-})\int\!\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{k}}_{T}\,\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{p}}_{T}\,\delta^{(2)}\bigl({\vec{q}}_{T}+{\vec{k}}_{T}-{\vec{p}}_{T}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\omega(\vec{k}_{T},\vec{p}_{T})\,g_{f}(x,k_{T})\,D_{f}(z,p_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})\,,

(5.12)

where
w({\vec{k}}_{T},{\vec{p}}_{T})
is weight factor dependent on
{\vec{k}}_{T}
and
{\vec{p}}_{T}
.
Explicit results for evaluating
\mathcal{F}[\omega\,\mathcal{H}\,g^{(n)}\,D^{(m)}]

in terms of eq. (
5
) are given in
appendix
B
.
Applying this to eq. (
5.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle W_{UU,T}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\left[\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,f_{1}D_{1}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{UU}^{\cos 2\phi_{h}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\left[\frac{-2k_{Tx}p_{Tx}+{\vec{k}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}{M_{N}M_{h}}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,h_{1}^{\perp}H_{1}^{\perp}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{UL}^{\sin 2\phi_{h}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\left[\frac{-2k_{Tx}p_{Tx}+{\vec{k}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}{M_{N}M_{h}}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,h_{1L}^{\perp}H_{1}^{\perp}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{LL}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\left[\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,g_{1L}D_{1}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{UT,T}^{\sin\left(\phi_{h}-\phi_{S}\right)}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\left[-\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,f_{1T}^{\perp}D_{1}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{UT}^{\sin\left(\phi_{h}+\phi_{S}\right)}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\left[-\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,h_{1}H_{1}^{\perp}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{UT}^{\sin\left(3\phi_{h}-\phi_{S}\right)}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\left[\frac{2k_{Tx}\,(\vec{k}_{T}\cdot\vec{p}_{T})+k_{T}^{2}\,p_{Tx}-4k_{Tx}^{2}\,p_{Tx}}{2M_{N}^{2}M_{h}}\,\mathcal{H}^{(0)}h_{1T}^{\perp}H_{1}^{\perp}\right]\,,

\displaystyle W_{LT}^{\cos\left(\phi_{h}-\phi_{S}\right)}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\left[\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,g_{1T}D_{1}\right]\,,

(5.13)

where we work in a frame where
{\vec{q}}_{T}=(-q_{T},0)
.

5.2
Next-to-Leading Power

In this section, we derive factorization formulas for the SIDIS structure functions at NLP.
We again reiterate that in this work we have neglected the impact of leading power Glauber exchange, which spoils factorization. Effectively, if these contributions can be shown to cancel like they do at LP, then we have derived in this work the complete form of the NLP factorization theorems for SIDIS.
We have considered all possible sources of power corrections, namely kinematic corrections, subleading hard scattering currents generated at both the hard and hard-collinear scales, and subleading
{\rm SCET}_{\rm II}
Lagrangian insertions. The required operators were discussed and derived in
section
4.1
.

Of these NLP effects, all terms that involve time-ordered products in
{\rm SCET}_{\rm II}
were shown to vanish in
section
4.4
, and all other terms that could generate subleading power soft functions were explicitly demonstrated to vanish in
section
4.5
.

The non-vanishing effects identified at NLP include kinematic corrections, given in
section
4.3
, and contributions from
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
and
\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}
operators, given in
section
4.6
and
section
4.7
, respectively. We setup the general Lorentz decompositions of the subleading power matrix elements and the contractions needed to obtain the NLP structure functions in the following sections:
kinematic corrections from expanding projectors in
section
5.2.1
,
corrections from
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
operators in
section
5.2.2
, and
corrections from operators with collinear
\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}
gluon insertions in
section
5.2.3
.
The combined final NLP structure function results for
W_{1,2,5,6}^{X}
with various spin-polarizations
X
are then presented in
section
5.2.4
in Fourier space and in
section
5.2.5
in momentum space. The translations between different notations for these structure functions was given above in eq. (
2.37
).
In this subsection, we will derive NLP factorization formulae for these structure functions, using similar methods as in the leading-power case.

Note that we have not fully determined (or eliminated) NLP corrections to structure functions start off at LP in eq. (
5.1
), namely
W_{-1,3,4,7}^{X}
. We have demonstrated that most sources of NLP power corrections
vanish for these structure functions, including: the kinematic corrections, the operators involving insertions of soft gluon or soft derivatives (
\mathcal{B}_{s\perp}^{(n_{i})\mu}
,
{\bar{n}}\cdot\mathcal{B}_{s}^{(n)}
,
n\cdot\mathcal{B}_{s}^{({\bar{n}})}
,
in\cdot\partial_{s}
, and
i{\bar{n}}\cdot\partial_{s}
), and the operators involving insertions of a
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}^{\mu}
or a collinear gluon field
\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}^{\mu}
. However we have not demonstrated that contributions from
{\rm SCET}_{\rm II}
Lagrangian insertions vanish for
W_{-1,3,4,7}^{X}
, nor have we shown that hard-collinear time-ordered products that arise from the
{\rm SCET}_{\rm I}
operator
{\cal O}_{I}^{(0)}
vanish.
Since the corresponding angular coefficients are dominated by the LP structure functions, potential NLP contributions here are of less phenomenological importance
than for those structure functions that start at NLP, which we do fully treat.
For the inclusive structure function,
W_{-1}^{U}
, we expect that no NLP corrections arise due to its azimuthal symmetry in
\phi_{h}
, which is akin to the discussion for unpolarized Drell-Yan
(see for example the discussion in ref.
[
45
]
).

5.2.1
Kinematic Corrections

As discussed in
section
4.3
, only three of the projectors
P_{i}^{\mu\nu}

defined in eq. (
2.2.1
) yield
\mathcal{O}(\lambda)
corrections when contracted with
the LP hadronic tensor
W_{\mu\nu}^{(0)}
, namely
P_{1}^{\mu\nu},P_{5}^{\mu\nu}
and
P_{6}^{\mu\nu}

as given in eq. (
4.3
). By contracting these with the Dirac structures appearing in eq. (
5.9
),
we obtain the induced power corrections. Here, we only calculate the corresponding Dirac contractions, leaving the full summary of the results for structure functions to sections
5.2.4
and
5.2.5
below.
Eq. (
5.9
) contains the Dirac structures

\displaystyle\Bigl\{g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}\,,\quad\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}\,,\quad\frac{g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}}{2}-\frac{b_{\perp}^{\mu}b_{\perp}^{\nu}}{b_{\perp}^{2}}\,,\quad b_{\perp}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}+b_{\perp}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\,,\quad b_{\perp}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}S_{\perp\rho}+S_{\perp}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\Bigr\}\,.

(5.14)

The nonvanishing contractions with eq. (
4.3
) are given by

\displaystyle P_{1\,\mu\nu}g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{T}}{Q}\,,

(5.15a)

\displaystyle P_{6\,\mu\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\frac{q_{T}}{Q}\,,

(5.15b)

\displaystyle P_{1\,\mu\nu}\left(\frac{g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}}{2}-\frac{b_{\perp}^{\mu}b_{\perp}^{\nu}}{b_{\perp}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{q_{T}}{2Q}\cos(2\varphi)\,,

\displaystyle P_{5\,\mu\nu}\left(\frac{g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}}{2}-\frac{b_{\perp}^{\mu}b_{\perp}^{\nu}}{b_{\perp}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{q_{T}}{2Q}\sin(2\varphi)\,,

(5.15c)

\displaystyle P_{1\,\mu\nu}\left(b_{\perp}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}+b_{\perp}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\right)

\displaystyle=-b_{\perp}^{2}\frac{q_{T}}{Q}\sin(2\varphi)\,,

\displaystyle P_{5\,\mu\nu}\left(b_{\perp}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}+b_{\perp}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\right)

\displaystyle=+b_{\perp}^{2}\frac{q_{T}}{Q}\cos(2\varphi)\,,

(5.15d)

\displaystyle P_{1\,\mu\nu}\left(b_{\perp}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}S_{\perp\rho}+S_{\perp}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{T}b_{T}}{Q}\bigl(S_{Tx}\sin\varphi+S_{Ty}\cos\varphi\bigr)\,,

(5.15e)

\displaystyle P_{5\,\mu\nu}\left(b_{\perp}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}S_{\perp\rho}+S_{\perp}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{T}b_{T}}{Q}\bigl(-S_{Tx}\cos\varphi+S_{Ty}\sin\varphi\bigr)\,.

(5.15f)

The contraction given by the fourth equality (second line of eq. (
5.15
)) does not appear in our final results,
as in eq. (
5.9
) the traceless tensor only multiplies spin-independent terms,
but all structure functions in eq. (
2.37
) defined from
P_{5}
involve spin structures.

5.2.2
Contributions from the
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
Operators

As discussed in
section
4.6
, the
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
operators introduce new
subleading correlators, which at NLP accuracy are defined in eq. (
4.6
). At NLP they were proven to be completely determined by the LP TMD PDFs and TMD FFs. A key step in this proof was demonstrating that the same LP soft function could be absorbed into these correlators, since doing so only induces a NNLP correction that we then can neglect.
The corresponding expressions in momentum space can be obtained
by applying eq. (
B
) and integrating by parts,

\displaystyle B_{\mathcal{P}\,f/N}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{b}}_{T}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{+\mathrm{i}{\vec{b}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{k}}_{T}}\frac{-\mathrm{i}}{2Q}\frac{\partial}{\partial b_{\perp}^{\rho}}\left[\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}\,\not{{\bar{n}}}\,,\,B_{f/N}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})\right]=\frac{1}{2Q}\left[B_{f/N}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})\,,\,\not{{\bar{n}}}\not{k}_{\perp}\right]\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{P}\,h/f}(z,{\vec{p}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{b}}_{T}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-\mathrm{i}{\vec{b}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2Q}\frac{\partial}{\partial b_{\perp}^{\rho}}\left[\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}\,\not{n}\,,\,\mathcal{G}_{h/f}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})\right]=\frac{1}{2Q}\left[\mathcal{G}_{h/f}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})\,,\,\not{n}\not{p}_{\perp}\right]\,,

(5.16)

where we assumed that the correlator vanishes sufficiently fast as
b_{T}\to\infty

as stated by the Riemann-Lebesgue Lemma.

One advantage of performing this Fourier transform is that it allows us to
use integration by parts to avoid derivatives of the LP correlators.
In
b_{T}
space, this can also be done, but only at the level of the hadronic tensor.
Furthermore, it allows us to compare our results for
B_{\mathcal{P}\,f/N}
and
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{P}\,h/f}

to the NLP terms in the quark-quark correlators in the literature.
By inserting eqs. (
5.1
) and (
5.4
) into eq. (
5.2.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle B_{\mathcal{P}\,f/N}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2Q}\biggl\{f_{1}\not{k}_{\perp}-f_{1T}^{\perp}\frac{\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}k_{\perp\rho}S_{\perp\sigma}}{M_{N}}\not{k}_{\perp}-g_{1L}\,S_{L}\not{k}_{\perp}\gamma_{5}+g_{1T}\frac{k_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}}{M_{N}}\not{k}_{\perp}\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{2}h_{1}\Bigl([\not{S}_{\perp},\not{k}_{\perp}]+\frac{1}{2}k_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}[\not{n},\not{{\bar{n}}}]\Bigr)\gamma_{5}+h_{1L}^{\perp}\,S_{L}\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{4M_{N}}[\not{n},\not{{\bar{n}}}]\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+h_{1T}^{\perp}\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{4M_{N}^{2}}\Bigl([\not{S}_{\perp},\not{k}_{\perp}]-\frac{1}{2}k_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}[\not{n},\not{{\bar{n}}}]\Bigr)\gamma_{5}+\mathrm{i}h_{1}^{\perp}\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{4M_{N}}[\not{n},\not{{\bar{n}}}]\biggr\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{P}\,h/f}(z,{\vec{p}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2Q}\biggl\{D_{1}\not{p}_{\perp}-\mathrm{i}H_{1}^{\perp}\frac{p_{\perp}^{2}}{4M_{h}}\bigl[\not{n},\not{{\bar{n}}}\bigr]\biggr\}\,.

(5.17)

This produces the
{\vec{k}}_{T}
-dependent Dirac structure of the quark-quark correlators at twist-3 in Ref.
[
21
]
.
Fourier transforming eq. (
5.2.2
) yields the corresponding results in
{\vec{b}}_{T}
space,

\displaystyle B_{\mathcal{P}\,f/N}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{N}}{2Q}\biggl\{-\mathrm{i}M_{N}f_{1}^{(1)}\not{b}_{\perp}+\Big[\frac{M_{N}^{2}}{2}f_{1T}^{\perp(2)}(b_{\perp\rho}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}S_{\perp\sigma})\not{b}_{\perp}+f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}\gamma_{\rho}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}S_{\perp\sigma}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathrm{i}M_{N}g_{1L}^{(1)}\,S_{L}\not{b}_{\perp}\gamma_{5}-\Bigl[\frac{M_{N}^{2}}{2}g_{1T}^{(2)}(b_{\perp}{\cdot}S_{\perp})\not{b}_{\perp}+g_{1T}^{(1)}\not{S}_{\perp}\Bigr]\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}M_{N}h_{1}^{(1)}\Bigl([\not{S}_{\perp},\not{b}_{\perp}]+\frac{1}{2}b_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}[\not{n},\not{{\bar{n}}}]\Bigr)\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\frac{S_{L}}{4}h_{1L}^{\perp\,(0^{\prime})}[\not{n},\not{{\bar{n}}}]\gamma_{5}-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{4}h_{1}^{\perp\,(0^{\prime})}[\not{n},\not{{\bar{n}}}]

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{4}h_{1T}^{\perp\,(1^{\prime})}M_{N}\Bigl([\not{S}_{\perp},\not{b}_{\perp}]-\frac{1}{2}b_{\perp}{\cdot}S_{\perp}[\not{n},\not{{\bar{n}}}]\Bigr)\gamma_{5}\biggr\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{P}\,h/f}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{h}}{2Q}\biggl\{\mathrm{i}D_{1}^{(1)}M_{h}\not{b}_{\perp}+\frac{\mathrm{i}}{4}H_{1}^{\perp\,(0^{\prime})}\bigl[\not{n},\not{{\bar{n}}}\bigr]\biggr\}\,.

(5.18)

Here, we made use of the abbreviations

\displaystyle f^{(0^{\prime})}(b_{T})

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{p}}_{T}\,e^{-\mathrm{i}{\vec{b}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}\frac{p_{T}^{2}}{M_{N}^{2}}f(p_{T})\,,

\displaystyle-\mathrm{i}M_{N}b_{\perp}^{\mu}f^{(1^{\prime})}(b_{T})

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{p}}_{T}\,e^{-\mathrm{i}{\vec{b}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}\frac{p_{\perp}^{\mu}p_{T}^{2}}{M_{N}^{3}}f(p_{T})\,.

(5.19)

see eq. (
B
) for explicit expressions in terms of the standard
f^{(n)}
.

To obtain the NLP contribution from
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
operators to the hadronic tensor,
we insert eq. (
5.2.2
) into eq. (
4.229
). Here, we only give the result for the resulting traces,
while the results for the structure functions will be given below in sections
5.2.4
and
5.2.5
.
We obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[B_{\mathcal{P}\,f/N}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})\,\gamma^{\mu}\,\mathcal{G}_{h/f}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})\,\gamma^{\nu}\right]

(5.20)

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{N}}{2Q}\biggl\{\Bigl(-\mathrm{i}f_{1}^{(1)}+\frac{M_{N}}{2}f_{1T}^{\perp(2)}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}b_{\perp\rho}S_{\perp\sigma}\Bigr)D_{1}\,M_{N}\left(b_{\perp}^{\mu}{\bar{n}}^{\nu}+{\bar{n}}^{\mu}b_{\perp}^{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt+f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}D_{1}\left({\bar{n}}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}S_{\perp\rho}+{\bar{n}}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}S_{\perp\rho}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98866pt-\left(S_{L}\,g_{1L}^{(1)}+\frac{\mathrm{i}M_{N}}{2}\,b_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}\,g_{1T}^{(2)}\right)D_{1}\,M_{N}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}{\bar{n}}_{\rho}b_{\perp\sigma}-\mathrm{i}g_{1T}^{(1)}D_{1}\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}{\bar{n}}_{\rho}S_{\perp\sigma}

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt-\mathrm{i}M_{h}h_{1}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\left(b_{\perp}^{\mu}{\bar{n}}^{\nu}+{\bar{n}}^{\mu}b_{\perp}^{\nu}\right)-\mathrm{i}M_{h}S_{L}h_{1L}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\left({\bar{n}}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}+{\bar{n}}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt+\frac{1}{2}M_{N}M_{h}\,h_{1T}^{\perp(1^{\prime})}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\bigl[b_{\perp}{\cdot}S_{\perp}\left({\bar{n}}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}+{\bar{n}}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\right)-\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}b_{\perp\rho}S_{\perp\sigma}\left({\bar{n}}^{\mu}b_{\perp}^{\nu}+{\bar{n}}^{\nu}b_{\perp}^{\mu}\right)\bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt+M_{N}M_{h}\,h_{1}^{(1)}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\bigl[b_{\perp}{\cdot}S_{\perp}\left({\bar{n}}^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}+{\bar{n}}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\right)+\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}b_{\perp\rho}S_{\perp\sigma}\left({\bar{n}}^{\mu}b_{\perp}^{\nu}+{\bar{n}}^{\nu}b_{\perp}^{\mu}\right)\bigr]\biggr\},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[B_{f/N}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})\,\gamma^{\mu}\,\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{P}\,h/f}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})\,\gamma^{\nu}\right]

(5.21)

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{h}}{2Q}\biggl\{\left(\mathrm{i}f_{1}-M_{N}f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}\,\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}b_{\perp\rho}S_{\perp\sigma}\right)D_{1}^{(1)}M_{h}\left(b_{\perp}^{\mu}n^{\nu}+n^{\mu}b_{\perp}^{\nu}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt-\left(S_{L}\,g_{1L}+\mathrm{i}M_{N}\,b_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}\,g_{1T}^{(1)}\right)D_{1}^{(1)}\,M_{h}n_{\rho}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}b_{\perp\sigma}

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt+\mathrm{i}M_{N}\,h_{1}^{\perp(1)}H_{1}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}\left(b_{\perp}^{\mu}n^{\nu}+n^{\mu}b_{\perp}^{\nu}\right)+\mathrm{i}M_{N}\,S_{L}h_{1L}^{\perp(1)}H_{1}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}\left(n^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}+n^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt+\frac{M_{N}^{2}}{2}\,h_{1T}^{\perp(2)}H_{1}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}\biggl[\frac{1}{2}b_{\perp}^{2}\bigl(n^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}S_{\perp\rho}+n^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}S_{\perp\rho}\bigr)-(b_{\perp}{\cdot}S_{\perp})\left(n^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}+n^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}b_{\perp\rho}\right)\biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 34.14322pt-h_{1}H_{1}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}\left(n^{\mu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\nu\rho}S_{\perp\rho}+n^{\nu}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\rho}S_{\perp\rho}\right)\biggr\}\,.

5.2.3
Contributions from the Collinear
\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}
Operators

The operators containing an insertion of a collinear gluon field give rise to the quark-gluon-quark correlators
in eq. (
4.7
). A key feature of this correlators is that the quark and gluon fields at the same transverse position
can freely exchange longitudinal momentum, and the correlator is sensitive to two momentum fractions.
This is distinct from similar quark-gluon-quark correlators studied already a long time ago in refs.
[
51
,
21
]
,
which only depend on the total momentum of the quark-gluon system.
20
20

20

Correlators with a distinct dependence on both momenta were found independently
in the recent work of ref.
[
96
]
.

Despite this difference, it will still be useful to employ the same Lorentz index decomposition of the quark-gluon-quark correlator
as in ref.
[
21
]
, up to including this additional momentum dependence.
21
21

21

In
appendix
D.3
, we provide more details on how to relate our
\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\rho}

and
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\rho}
to their
\tilde{\Phi}_{A}^{\rho}
and
\tilde{\Delta}_{A}^{\rho}
, respectively.
The key results are given in eqs. (
D.24
) and (
D.67
),
which relate our correlators
integrated over
\xi
and before the soft subtraction

to their purely hadronic correlators.

We define our correlators in momentum space as

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho}(x,\xi,{\vec{k}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{N}}{4P_{N}^{-}}\biggl\{\biggl[\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp}\frac{k_{\perp\sigma}}{M_{N}}-\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}_{T}\,\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}S_{\perp}^{\delta}-S_{L}\,\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}_{L}^{\perp}\frac{\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}\,k_{\perp}^{\delta}}{M_{N}}

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt-\frac{\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}_{T}^{\perp}}{M_{N}^{2}}\left({\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}S_{\perp}^{\delta}}-k_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}k_{\perp}^{\delta}\right)\biggr]\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}-\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt-\Bigl[S_{L}\,\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{L}-\frac{k_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}}{M_{N}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}\Bigr]\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}\,\gamma_{5}+\Bigl[\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}+\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}^{\perp}\,\frac{\epsilon_{\perp}^{\sigma\delta}k_{\perp\sigma}\,S_{\perp\delta}}{M_{N}}\,\Bigr]\mathrm{i}\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\ldots\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}+\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)\biggr\}\frac{\not{n}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{N}}{4P_{N}^{-}}\biggl\{\biggl[\bigl(\tilde{f}^{\perp}-\mathrm{i}\tilde{g}^{\perp}\bigr)\frac{k_{\perp\sigma}}{M_{N}}-\bigl(\tilde{f}_{T}+\mathrm{i}\tilde{g}_{T}\bigr)\,\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}S_{\perp}^{\delta}-S_{L}\bigl(\tilde{f}_{L}^{\perp}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{g}_{L}^{\perp}\bigr)\frac{\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}\,k_{\perp}^{\delta}}{M_{N}}

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt-\frac{\tilde{f}_{T}^{\perp}+\mathrm{i}\tilde{g}_{T}^{\perp}}{M_{N}^{2}}\left({\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2}\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}S_{\perp}^{\delta}}-k_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}k_{\perp}^{\delta}\right)\biggr]\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}-\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt-\Bigl[S_{L}\bigl(\tilde{h}_{L}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{e}_{L}\bigr)-\frac{k_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}}{M_{N}}\bigl(\tilde{h}_{T}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{e}_{T}\bigr)\Bigr]\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}\,\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\Bigl[\bigl(\tilde{h}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{e}\bigr)+\bigl(\tilde{h}_{T}^{\perp}-\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{e}_{T}^{\perp}\bigr)\,\frac{\epsilon_{\perp}^{\sigma\delta}k_{\perp\sigma}\,S_{\perp\delta}}{M_{N}}\,\Bigr]\mathrm{i}\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\ldots\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}+\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)\biggr\}\frac{\not{n}}{2}\,,

(5.22)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}^{\rho}(z,\xi,{\vec{p}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{h}}{4P_{h}^{+}}\,\biggl\{\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp}\,\frac{p_{\perp\sigma}}{M_{h}}\,\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}-\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)+\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\,\mathrm{i}\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}+\ldots\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}+\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)\biggr\}\frac{\not{{\bar{n}}}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{h}}{4P_{h}^{+}}\,\biggl\{\bigl(\tilde{D}^{\perp}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{G}^{\perp}\bigr)\,\frac{p_{\perp\sigma}}{M_{h}}\,\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}-\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\bigl(\tilde{H}-\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{E}\bigr)\,\mathrm{i}\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}+\ldots\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}+\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)\biggr\}\frac{\not{{\bar{n}}}}{2}\,.

(5.23)

We have defined the complex function
\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp}\equiv\tilde{f}^{\perp}-\mathrm{i}\tilde{g}^{\perp}
, and similarly for other cases as indicated above.
For brevity, we suppress the arguments on the right-hand side,
i.e. we have the arguments

\tilde{f}^{\perp}\equiv\tilde{f}^{\perp}_{f/N}(x,\xi,k_{T},\mu,\zeta_{a})
and

\tilde{D}^{\perp}\equiv\tilde{D}^{\perp}_{h/f}(z,\xi,p_{T},\mu,\zeta_{b})
,
and likewise for all other TMDs. We have also suppressed the dependence on the renormalization scale
\mu
and Collins-Soper parameters
\zeta_{a,b}
on the left hand side.
The terms in ellipses in eqs. (
5.2.3
) and (
5.2.3
)
do not contribute to the SIDIS structure functions
[
21
]
.
22
22

22

Compared to the notation for the Lorentz decomposition in
[
21
]
, we have already made use in our eq. (
5.2.3
) of the relations

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{T}=\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{T}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2M_{N}^{2}}\tilde{f}_{T}^{\perp}\,,\quad\tilde{g}_{T}=\tilde{g}^{\prime}_{T}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2M_{N}^{2}}\tilde{g}_{T}^{\perp}\,,

(5.24)

and hence do not define
\tilde{f}^{\prime}_{T}
or
\tilde{g}^{\prime}_{T}
here.

We stress that while the structure of eqs. (
5.2.3
) and (
5.2.3
)
is consistent with that obtained by applying eqs. (
D.24
) and (
D.67
)
to decomposition in ref.
[
21
]
, a key difference is that here all TMDs here are defined after absorbing the LP soft function as in eq. (
4.7
),
and with an extra longitudinal momentum fraction
\xi
dependence that was not present in ref.
[
21
]
.

For the antiquark contribution, analogous to eq. (
5.1
), our quark-gluon-quark correlator satisfies the following relations,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[\sigma_{\rho+}\,\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,\bar{f}/N}^{\rho}(x,\xi,{\vec{k}}_{T})\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[\sigma_{\rho+}\,\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho\,c}(x,\xi,{\vec{k}}_{T})\bigr]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[\mathrm{i}\sigma_{\rho+}\gamma_{5}\,\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,\bar{f}/N}^{\rho}(x,\xi,{\vec{k}}_{T})\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[\mathrm{i}\sigma_{\rho+}\gamma_{5}\,\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho\,c}(x,\xi,{\vec{k}}_{T})\bigr]\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\sigma\rho}+\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\sigma\rho}\gamma_{5}\bigr)\gamma^{+}\,\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,\bar{f}/N,\,\rho}(x,\xi,{\vec{k}}_{T})\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\bigl[\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\sigma\rho}-\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\sigma\rho}\gamma_{5}\bigr)\gamma^{+}\,\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N,\,\rho}^{c}(x,\xi,{\vec{k}}_{T})\bigr]\,.

(5.25)

Here,
\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho\,c}
is
\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho}
evaluated with charge-conjugated fields
\chi^{c}=C\bar{\chi}^{T}
and
\mathcal{B}_{n\perp\,\rho}^{c}=-\mathcal{B}_{n\perp\,\rho}^{T}
.
Notice that
\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho\,c}
then (roughly) corresponds to the hermitian conjugate of
\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho}
, namely, flipping sign of every
\mathrm{i}
in eq. (
5.2.3
) except the one in
\mathrm{i}\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}
. Therefore, the decomposition of
\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,\bar{f}/N}^{\rho}
is identical to eq. (
5.2.3
), except that
\tilde{g}^{\perp}
,
\tilde{g}_{T}
,
\tilde{g}^{\perp}_{L}
,
\tilde{g}^{\perp}_{T}
,
\tilde{e}_{L}
,
\tilde{e}_{T}
,
\tilde{e}
and
\tilde{e}^{\perp}_{T}
would receive a sign flip. Similarly,
\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/\bar{f}}^{\rho}(z,\xi,{\vec{p}}_{T})
has the same decomposition as eq. (
5.2.3
) except that
\tilde{G}
and
\tilde{E}
have a sign flip.

The Fourier transforms of eqs. (
5.2.3
) and (
5.2.3
) can be obtained
in the usual fashion using the results in
appendix
B
:

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{N}}{4P_{N}^{-}}\biggl\{\biggl[-\mathrm{i}M_{N}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp(1)}\,b_{\perp\sigma}-\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}_{T}\,\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}S_{\perp}^{\delta}+\mathrm{i}M_{N}S_{L}\,\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}_{L}^{\perp(1)}\,\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}b_{\perp}^{\delta}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}M_{N}^{2}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}_{T}^{\perp(2)}\,\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2}b_{\perp}^{2}S_{\perp}^{\delta}-b_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}b_{\perp}^{\delta}\Bigr)\biggr]\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}-\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left[S_{L}\,\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{L}+\mathrm{i}M_{N}\,b_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}\,\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}^{(1)}\right]\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}\,\gamma_{5}+\Bigl[\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}-\mathrm{i}M_{N}\,\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}^{\perp(1)}\,\epsilon_{\perp}^{\sigma\delta}b_{\perp\sigma}\,S_{\perp\delta}\,\Bigr]\mathrm{i}\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\ldots\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}+\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)\biggr\}\frac{\not{n}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{N}}{4P_{N}^{-}}\biggl\{\biggl[-\mathrm{i}M_{N}\bigl(\tilde{f}^{\perp(1)}-\mathrm{i}\tilde{g}^{\perp(1)}\bigr)b_{\perp\sigma}-\bigl(\tilde{f}_{T}+\mathrm{i}\tilde{g}_{T}\bigr)\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}S_{\perp}^{\delta}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathrm{i}M_{N}S_{L}\bigl(\tilde{f}_{L}^{\perp(1)}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{g}_{L}^{\perp(1)}\bigr)\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}b_{\perp}^{\delta}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{1}{2}M_{N}^{2}\bigl(\tilde{f}_{T}^{\perp(2)}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{g}_{T}^{\perp(2)}\bigr)\epsilon_{\perp\sigma\delta}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2}b_{\perp}^{2}S_{\perp}^{\delta}-b_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}b_{\perp}^{\delta}\Bigr)\biggr]\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}-\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt-\left[S_{L}\bigl(\tilde{h}_{L}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{e}_{L}\bigr)+\mathrm{i}M_{N}\,b_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}\bigl(\tilde{h}_{T}^{(1)}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{e}_{T}^{(1)}\bigr)\right]\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}\,\gamma_{5}

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\Bigl[\bigl(\tilde{h}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{e}\bigr)-\mathrm{i}M_{N}\bigl(\tilde{h}_{T}^{\perp(1)}-\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{e}_{T}^{\perp(1)}\bigr)\,\epsilon_{\perp}^{\sigma\delta}b_{\perp\sigma}\,S_{\perp\delta}\,\Bigr]\mathrm{i}\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\ldots\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}+\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)\biggr\}\frac{\not{n}}{2}\,,

(5.26a)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}^{\rho}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{h}}{4P_{h}^{+}}\Bigl\{\mathrm{i}M_{h}\,\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}b_{\perp\sigma}\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}-\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)+\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\,\mathrm{i}\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}+\ldots\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}+\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\alpha\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)\Bigr\}\frac{\not{{\bar{n}}}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{h}}{4P_{h}^{+}}\Bigl\{\mathrm{i}M_{h}\bigl(\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{G}^{\perp(1)}\bigr)b_{\perp\sigma}\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}-\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\bigl(\tilde{H}-\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{E}\bigr)\,\mathrm{i}\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}+\ldots\bigl(g_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}+\mathrm{i}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\alpha\sigma}\gamma_{5}\bigr)\Bigr\}\frac{\not{{\bar{n}}}}{2}\,.

(5.26b)

Here, the
f^{(n)}
are defined in eq. (
5.1
) as usual.

To obtain the contributions from these correlators to the NLP hadronic tensor
as given by eq. (
4.7
), we require the traces

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left[\tilde{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho}\gamma_{\perp}^{\mu}\mathcal{G}_{h/f}\gamma_{\perp\rho}+B_{f/N}\gamma_{\perp}^{\mu}\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}^{\rho}\gamma_{\perp\rho}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{M_{N}}{2P_{N}^{-}}\,\bigg\{-\mathrm{i}M_{N}\left(\tilde{f}^{\perp(1)}-\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{g}^{\perp(1)}\right)D_{1}\,b_{\perp}^{\mu}-\bigl(\tilde{f}_{T}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{g}_{T}\bigr)D_{1}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\sigma}S_{\perp\sigma}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathrm{i}M_{N}S_{L}\bigl(\tilde{f}_{L}^{\perp(1)}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{g}_{L}^{\perp(1)}\bigr)D_{1}\,\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\sigma}\,b_{\perp\sigma}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{M_{N}^{2}}{2}\,\bigl(\tilde{f}_{T}^{\perp(2)}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{g}_{T}^{\perp(2)}\bigr)D_{1}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\sigma}\Bigl[\frac{1}{2}b_{\perp}^{2}S_{\perp\sigma}-(b_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp})\,b_{\perp\sigma}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathrm{i}M_{h}\left[S_{L}\bigl(\tilde{h}_{L}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{e}_{L}\bigr)+\mathrm{i}M_{N}\,b_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}\bigl(\tilde{h}_{T}^{(1)}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{e}_{T}^{(1)}\bigr)\right]H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\sigma}b_{\perp\sigma}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathrm{i}M_{h}\left[\left(\tilde{h}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{e}\right)-\mathrm{i}M_{N}\left(\tilde{h}_{T}^{\perp(1)}-\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{e}_{T}^{\perp(1)}\right)\,\epsilon_{\perp}^{\sigma\delta}b_{\perp\sigma}\,S_{\perp\delta}\right]H_{1}^{\perp(1)}b_{\perp}^{\mu}\bigg\}

\displaystyle-\frac{M_{h}}{2P_{h}^{+}}\,\bigg\{\mathrm{i}M_{h}\left(f_{1}+\mathrm{i}M_{N}f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\sigma\delta}b_{\perp\sigma}S_{\perp\delta}\right)\left(\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}+\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{G}^{\perp(1)}\right)b_{\perp}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\mathrm{i}M_{h}\left(S_{L}g_{1L}+\mathrm{i}M_{N}\,b_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}\,g_{1T}^{(1)}\right)\left(\tilde{G}^{\perp(1)}-\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}\right)\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\sigma}\,b_{\perp\sigma}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\Bigl[-\mathrm{i}M_{N}\,h_{1}^{\perp(1)}b_{\perp}^{\mu}-\mathrm{i}M_{N}S_{L}h_{1L}^{\perp(1)}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\sigma}\,b_{\perp\sigma}+h_{1}\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\sigma}S_{\perp\sigma}\Bigr]\bigl(\tilde{H}-\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{E}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\frac{M_{N}^{2}}{2}h_{1T}^{\perp(2)}\bigl(\tilde{H}-\mathrm{i}\,\tilde{E}\bigr)\,\epsilon_{\perp}^{\mu\sigma}\Bigl[\frac{1}{2}b_{\perp}^{2}S_{\perp\sigma}-(b_{\perp}{\cdot}S_{\perp})\,b_{\perp\sigma}\Bigr]\biggr\}\,.

(5.27)

This can be inserted into eq. (
4.7
) to obtain the NLP contributions
to the hadronic tensor arising from an additional gluon field
\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}
.

5.2.4
Combined Results in Fourier Space

In the previous sections, we provided all the ingredients required to calculate
the individual contributions to the subleading structure functions from

•

Kinematic corrections
in
section
5.2.1
:
contract the leading-power hadronic tensor in eq. (
4.2
)
with the subleading projectors in eq. (
4.3
) to obtain
P^{(1)}_{i\,\mu\nu}W^{(0)\,\mu\nu}
.
The contractions to take into account are given in eq. (
5.15
).

•

\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
operators
in
section
5.2.2
:
insert eqs. (
5.20
) and (
5.21
) into eq. (
4.229
) to obtain
W_{\mathcal{P}}^{(1)\,\mu\nu}
,
and contract with the LP projectors to obtain
P^{(0)}_{i\,\mu\nu}W_{\mathcal{P}}^{(1)\,\mu\nu}

•

\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}
operators
in
section
5.2.3
:
insert eq. (
5.2.3
) into eq. (
4.7
) to obtain
W_{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)\,\mu\nu}
,
and contract with the LP projectors to obtain
P^{(0)}_{i\,\mu\nu}W_{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)\,\mu\nu}

.

By adding these contributions, we obtain the subleading structure functions as

\displaystyle W_{i}={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}P^{(1)}_{i\,\mu\nu}W^{(0)\,\mu\nu}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}P^{(0)}_{i\,\mu\nu}W_{\mathcal{P}}^{(1)\,\mu\nu}}+{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}P^{(0)}_{i\,\mu\nu}W_{\mathcal{B}}^{(1)\,\mu\nu}}\,.

(5.28)

By separating
W_{i}
according to eq. (
2.2.3
) into the different spin structures,
we obtain the individual
W_{i}^{U}
,
W_{i}^{L}
,
W_{i}^{Tx}
and
W_{i}^{Ty}
.
Combining these following eq. (
2.37
) then yields the polarized structure functions at NLP.
In the following results, we will always use the above color scheme
to indicate the different sources of power corrections,
except when we combine the individual pieces to obtain more compact final results.

Interestingly,
W_{LU}^{\sin\phi_{h}}
is the only structure function that does not receive kinematic corrections
and contributions from
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
operators, and hence only has
\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}
contributions. All the other structure functions receive contributions from all three sources of power corrections.

At leading power, we expressed all structure functions in terms of the Fourier transform in eq. (
5.1
).
While we still encounter the same Fourier transform for (some of) the kinematic and
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
corrections,
the
\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}
operators give rise an additional convolution in
\xi
.
Thus, we have to slightly adapt
\mathcal{F}
at NLP, and define three versions of it, which varies depending on which functions it acts:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{H}\,g^{(n)}D^{(m)}]

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-})\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}b_{T}\,b_{T}}{2\pi}(M_{N}b_{T})^{n}(-M_{h}b_{T})^{m}J_{n+m}(b_{T}q_{T})\,

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\,g_{f}^{(n)}(x,b_{T})\,D_{f}^{(m)}(z,b_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{H}\,\tilde{g}^{(n)}D^{(m)}]

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\int\!\mathrm{d}\xi\,\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-},\xi)\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}b_{T}\,b_{T}}{2\pi}(M_{N}b_{T})^{n}(-M_{h}b_{T})^{m}J_{n+m}(b_{T}q_{T})\,

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\,\tilde{g}_{f}^{(n)}(x,\xi,b_{T})\,D_{f}^{(m)}(z,b_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{H}\,g^{(n)}\tilde{D}^{(m)}]

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\int\!\mathrm{d}\xi\,\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-},\xi)\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}b_{T}\,b_{T}}{2\pi}(M_{N}b_{T})^{n}(-M_{h}b_{T})^{m}J_{n+m}(b_{T}q_{T})\,

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\,g_{f}^{(n)}(x,b_{T})\,\tilde{D}_{f}^{(m)}(z,\xi,b_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})\,.

(5.29)

Here, the
f^{(n)}
are as usual defined as the Fourier transforms in eq. (
B
).
The definition in the first line of eq. (
5.2.4
) is identical to eq. (
5.1
).
In the second (third) line, there is a convolution over
\xi
between the hard function
\mathcal{H}

and the quark-gluon-quark TMDPDF
\tilde{g}
(quark-gluon-quark TMDFF
\tilde{D}
), as indicated by the explicit arguments for these TMDs.
Since the distinction is unique from the arguments, we use the same symbol
\mathcal{F}
in all cases.
The functions
f^{(0^{\prime})}
and
f^{(1^{\prime})}
defined in eq. (
5.2.2
) are treated as
n=0
and
n=1
, respectively.
Note that on the RHS of eq. (
5.2.4
) we have once again suppressed important dependence on the renormalization scale
\mu
, and Collins-Soper scales
\zeta_{a,b}
, which can be restored by writing
\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-},\mu)
,
\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-},\xi,\mu)
,
g_{f}^{(n)}(x,b_{T},\mu,\zeta_{a})
,

\tilde{g}_{f}^{(n)}(x,\xi,b_{T},\mu,\zeta_{a})
,

D_{f}^{(m)}(z,b_{T},\mu,\zeta_{b})
, and
\tilde{D}_{f}^{(m)}(z,\xi,b_{T},\mu,\zeta_{b})
.

At LP, the above Fourier transform was sufficient, as all powers of
b_{T}

were compensated by a corresponding factor of
\cos\varphi
, where
\cos\varphi=-{\vec{b}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{q}}_{T}/(b_{T}q_{T})
,
leading to the particularly simple form of eq. (
5.2.4
) as an integral over
J_{n+m}
.
At NLP, this is not true anymore, and we encounter powers of
b_{T}

without the corresponding
\cos\varphi
. To handle these, we also define the modified Fourier transform

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime}[\mathcal{H}\,g^{(n)}\,D^{(m)}]

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-})\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{b}}_{T}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-\mathrm{i}{\vec{q}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{b}}_{T}}(-\mathrm{i}M_{N}b_{T})^{n}(\mathrm{i}M_{h}b_{T})^{m}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\,g_{f}^{(n)}(x,b_{T})\,D_{f}^{(m)}(z,b_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-})\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}b_{T}\,b_{T}}{2\pi}(-\mathrm{i}M_{N}b_{T})^{n}(\mathrm{i}M_{h}b_{T})^{m}J_{0}(b_{T}q_{T})\,

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\,g_{f}^{(n)}(x,b_{T})\,D_{f}^{(m)}(z,b_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime}[\mathcal{H}\,\tilde{g}^{(n)}D^{(m)}]

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\int\!\mathrm{d}\xi\,\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-},\xi)\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}b_{T}\,b_{T}}{2\pi}(-\mathrm{i}M_{N}b_{T})^{n}(\mathrm{i}M_{h}b_{T})^{m}J_{0}(b_{T}q_{T})\,

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\,\tilde{g}_{f}^{(n)}(x,\xi,b_{T})\,D_{f}^{(m)}(z,b_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\prime}[\mathcal{H}\,g^{(n)}\tilde{D}^{(m)}]

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\int\!\mathrm{d}\xi\,\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-},\xi)\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\mathrm{d}b_{T}\,b_{T}}{2\pi}(-\mathrm{i}M_{N}b_{T})^{n}(\mathrm{i}M_{h}b_{T})^{m}J_{0}(b_{T}q_{T})\,

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\times\,g_{f}^{(n)}(x,b_{T})\,\tilde{D}_{f}^{(m)}(z,\xi,b_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})\,.

(5.30)

For
n=m=0
,
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}
coincide.

In the following we provide all structure functions that
start
at NLP,
since as previously mentioned we do not consider NLP corrections to structure functions already contributing at LP.
Here, we only provide expressions in position space.
Explicit results in momentum space will be presented in
section
5.2.5
.

Note that the structure functions
W_{LU}^{\sin\phi_{h}}
,
W_{LL}^{\cos\phi_{h}}
,
W_{LT}^{\cos\phi_{S}}
and
W_{LT}^{\cos\left(2\phi_{h}-\phi_{S}\right)}

arise from the antisymmetric projector
P_{2}^{\mu\nu}
and
P_{6}^{\mu\nu}
, where exchanging
\mu\leftrightarrow\nu

for the antiquark contribution in
W^{\mu\nu}
yields a relative minus sign. Since the corresponding
antiquark distributions
g_{1L}
,
g_{1T}
, and all the functions mentioned below eq. (
5.2.3
) also receive a minus sign of their own,
simply summing over all quarks and antiquarks as stated in eqs. (
5.2.4
) and (
5.2.4
) is correct.
Below we use the notation
\Re
and
\Im
for the real and imaginary parts respectively.

Unpolarized structure functions.

\displaystyle W_{UU}^{\cos\phi_{h}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{q_{T}}{Q}\,\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\left[-f_{1}D_{1}+h_{1}^{\perp(1)}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right]}

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}+\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\left[-\frac{M_{N}}{Q}f_{1}^{(1)}D_{1}-\frac{M_{h}}{Q}f_{1}D_{1}^{(1)}+\frac{M_{N}}{Q}h_{1}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}+\frac{M_{h}}{Q}h_{1}^{\perp(1)}H_{1}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}\right]}\biggl\}

\displaystyle\quad-\Re\biggl[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\left[\frac{2xM_{N}}{Q}\left(\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp(1)}D_{1}+\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}\,H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right)+\frac{2M_{h}}{zQ}\left(f_{1}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}+h_{1}^{\perp(1)}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\right)\right]}\biggl]\biggr\}\,,

(5.31)

\displaystyle W_{LU}^{\sin\phi_{h}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}\biggl\{-\Im\biggl[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\left[\frac{2xM_{N}}{Q}\left(\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp(1)}D_{1}+\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}\,H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right)+\frac{2M_{h}}{zQ}\left(f_{1}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}+h_{1}^{\perp(1)}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\right)\right]}\biggr]\biggr\}\,.

(5.32)

Longitudinally polarized structure functions.

\displaystyle W_{UL}^{\sin\phi_{h}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{q_{T}}{Q}\,\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,h_{1L}^{\perp(1)}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}+\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\left(\frac{M_{N}}{Q}h_{1L}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}+\frac{M_{h}}{Q}h_{1L}^{\perp(1)}H_{1}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Re\biggl[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\left[\frac{2xM_{N}}{Q}\left(\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp(1)}_{L}D_{1}-\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{L}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right)-\frac{2M_{h}}{zQ}\left(-\mathrm{i}g_{1L}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}+h_{1L}^{\perp(1)}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\right)\right]}\biggr]\biggr\}\,,

(5.33)

\displaystyle W_{LL}^{\cos\phi_{h}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-\frac{q_{T}}{Q}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}g_{1L}D_{1}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\left(\frac{M_{N}}{Q}\,g_{1L}^{(1)}D_{1}+\frac{M_{h}}{Q}\,g_{1L}D_{1}^{(1)}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad-\Im\biggl[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\left[\frac{2xM_{N}}{Q}\left(\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp(1)}_{L}D_{1}-\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{L}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right)-\frac{2M_{h}}{zQ}\left(-\mathrm{i}g_{1L}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}+h_{1L}^{\perp(1)}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\right)\right]}\biggr]\biggr\}\,.

(5.34)

Transversely polarized structure functions.

\displaystyle W_{UT}^{\sin\phi_{S}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-\frac{q_{T}}{2Q}\,\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\left(f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}D_{1}-2h_{1}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right)}\biggr\}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\left(-\frac{M_{N}}{2Q}f_{1T}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}D_{1}-\frac{M_{h}}{2Q}f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}D_{1}^{(1)}+\frac{M_{h}}{Q}h_{1}H_{1}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}+\frac{M_{N}}{Q}\,h_{1}^{(1)}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Re\biggl[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\biggl[\frac{2xM_{N}}{Q}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}_{T}D_{1}-\frac{2M_{h}}{zQ}h_{1}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}-\frac{xM_{N}}{Q}\Bigl(\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}^{(1)}-\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}^{\perp(1)}\Bigr)H_{1}^{\perp(1)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}-\frac{M_{h}}{zQ}\left(-\mathrm{i}g_{1T}^{(1)}\,\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}+f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}\right)\biggr]}\biggr]\biggr\}\,,

(5.35)

\displaystyle W_{UT}^{\sin\left(2\phi_{h}-\phi_{S}\right)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{q_{T}}{2Q}\,\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\left[f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}D_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\,h_{1T}^{\perp(2)}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right]}

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}+\frac{1}{2Q}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\biggl[\frac{1}{2}M_{N}f_{1T}^{\perp(2)}D_{1}+M_{h}f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}D_{1}^{(1)}+M_{N}h_{1T}^{\perp(1^{\prime})}H_{1}^{\perp(1)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}+\frac{1}{2}M_{h}\,h_{1T}^{\perp(2)}H_{1}^{\perp(0^{\prime})}\biggr]}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Re\biggl[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\biggl[\frac{xM_{N}}{Q}\left(\frac{1}{2}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}_{T}^{\perp(2)}D_{1}-\bigl(\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}^{(1)}+\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}^{\perp(1)}\bigr)H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}-\frac{M_{h}}{zQ}\left(\frac{1}{2}h_{1T}^{\perp(2)}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}-\mathrm{i}g_{1T}^{(1)}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}-f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}\right)\biggr]}\biggr]\biggr\}\,,

(5.36)

\displaystyle W_{LT}^{\cos\phi_{S}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-\frac{q_{T}}{2Q}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,g_{1T}^{(1)}D_{1}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\left[-\frac{M_{N}}{2Q}g_{1T}^{(0^{\prime})}D_{1}-\frac{M_{h}}{2Q}g_{1T}^{(1)}D_{1}^{(1)}\right]}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Im\biggl[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\biggl[\frac{xM_{N}}{Q}\left(-2\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}_{T}D_{1}+\bigl(\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}^{(1)}-\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}^{\perp(1)}\bigr)H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}+\frac{M_{h}}{zQ}\left(2h_{1}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}-\mathrm{i}g_{1T}^{(1)}\,\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}+f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}\right)\biggr]}\biggr]\biggr\}\,,

(5.37)

\displaystyle W_{LT}^{\cos\left(2\phi_{h}-\phi_{S}\right)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-\frac{q_{T}}{2Q}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}g_{1T}^{(1)}D_{1}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}-~\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\left[\frac{M_{N}}{4Q}g_{1T}^{(2)}D_{1}+\frac{M_{h}}{2Q}g_{1T}^{(1)}D_{1}^{(1)}\right]}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Im\biggl[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\biggl[\frac{xM_{N}}{Q}\left(-\frac{1}{2}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}_{T}^{\perp(2)}D_{1}+\bigl(\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}^{(1)}+\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}^{\perp(1)}\bigr)H_{1}^{\perp(1)}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}+\frac{M_{h}}{zQ}\left(\frac{1}{2}h_{1T}^{\perp(2)}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}-\mathrm{i}g_{1T}^{(1)}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}-f_{1T}^{\perp(1)}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp(1)}\right)\biggr]}\biggr]\biggr\}\,.

(5.38)

5.2.5
Combined Results in Momentum Space

In
section
5.2.4
, we provided the full results in position space,
which leads to particularly compact formulas. Historically, it was more common
to express the factorization as a convolution in momentum space.
In particular, ref.
[
21
]
only provided results for the subleading
structure functions in SIDIS in momentum space and we will make a comparison to their results below in
section
5.4.1
.

In the following we therefore repeat our results
in momentum space. To so, we only need write the Fourier transforms
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}

defined in eqs. (
5.1
) and (
5.2.4
) in terms of explicit momentum space convolutions
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}

as defined in eq. (
5
). As before, we have to modify the meaning of
\tilde{\mathcal{F}}

to include the integral over
\xi
for the subleading TMDs, and define

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{F}}[\omega\,\mathcal{H}\,g\,D]

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-})\int\!\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{k}}_{T}\,\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{p}}_{T}\,\delta^{(2)}\bigl({\vec{q}}_{T}+{\vec{k}}_{T}-{\vec{p}}_{T}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\omega(\vec{k}_{T},\vec{p}_{T})\,g_{f}(x,k_{T})\,D_{f}(z,p_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{F}}[\omega\,\mathcal{H}\,\tilde{g}\,D]

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\int\!\mathrm{d}\xi\,\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-},\xi)\int\!\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{k}}_{T}\,\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{p}}_{T}\,\delta^{(2)}\bigl({\vec{q}}_{T}+{\vec{k}}_{T}-{\vec{p}}_{T}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\omega(\vec{k}_{T},\vec{p}_{T})\,\tilde{g}_{f}(x,\xi,k_{T})\,D_{f}(z,p_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{F}}[\omega\,\mathcal{H}\,g\,\tilde{D}]

\displaystyle=2z\sum_{f}\int\!\mathrm{d}\xi\,\mathcal{H}_{f}(q^{+}q^{-},\xi)\int\!\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{k}}_{T}\,\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{p}}_{T}\,\delta^{(2)}\bigl({\vec{q}}_{T}+{\vec{k}}_{T}-{\vec{p}}_{T}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\omega(\vec{k}_{T},\vec{p}_{T})\,g_{f}(x,k_{T})\,\tilde{D}_{f}(z,\xi,p_{T})+(f\to\bar{f})\,,

(5.39)

where the
\tilde{g}
and
\tilde{D}
are the subleading TMDs, respectively.
Explicit results for expressing all appearing Fourier transforms
\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{H}g^{(n)}D^{(m)}]
,

\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{H}\tilde{g}^{(n)}D^{(m)}]
and
\mathcal{F}[\mathcal{H}g^{(n)}\tilde{D}^{(m)}]

in terms of the convolutions in eq. (
5.2.5
) are provided in
appendix
B
.
Again on the RHS of eq. (
5.2.5
) we have suppressed dependence of the various functions on the renormalization scale
\mu
and Collins-Soper parameters
\zeta_{a,b}
, which can be restored in the same manner that we discussed above for the Fourier space results.

In the following, for each NLP structure function we first show the direct
Fourier transform from the results in
section
5.2.4
, where we
still use color coding to separate contributions from
kinematic corrections
,

\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
operators
, and

\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}
operators
.
Secondly, we provide simplified results by using that in our frame choice,

{\vec{k}}_{T}+{\vec{q}}_{T}={\vec{p}}_{T}
implies

\displaystyle q_{T}=k_{Tx}-p_{Tx}\,,\qquad 0=k_{Ty}-p_{Ty}\,.

(5.40)

(Recall that
{\vec{q}}_{T}=q_{T}(-1,0)
.)
Using eq. (
5.40
) gives us results that are written in a form similar to those in ref.
[
21
]
.

Unpolarized structure functions.

\displaystyle W_{UU}^{\cos\phi_{h}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{q_{T}}{Q}\,\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\biggl[-f_{1}D_{1}+\frac{2k_{Tx}p_{Tx}-{\vec{k}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}{M_{N}M_{h}}h_{1}^{\perp}H_{1}^{\perp}\biggr]}

(5.41)

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}+\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\biggl[-\frac{k_{Tx}+p_{Tx}}{Q}f_{1}D_{1}+\frac{k_{T}^{2}p_{Tx}+p_{T}^{2}k_{Tx}}{M_{N}M_{h}Q}h_{1}^{\perp}H_{1}^{\perp}\biggr]}

\displaystyle\qquad-\Re\biggl[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\left[\frac{2xM_{N}}{Q}\left(\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp}D_{1}+\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}\,H_{1}^{\perp}\right)+\frac{2M_{h}}{zQ}\left(\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}f_{1}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp}+\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}h_{1}^{\perp}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\right)\right]}\biggr]\biggl\}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\biggl\{\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\biggl[-\frac{2k_{Tx}}{Q}f_{1}D_{1}+\frac{2k_{T}^{2}p_{Tx}}{M_{N}M_{h}Q}h_{1}^{\perp}H_{1}^{\perp}\biggr]

\displaystyle\qquad-\Re\biggl[\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\left[\frac{2xM_{N}}{Q}\left(\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp}D_{1}+\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}\,H_{1}^{\perp}\right)+\frac{2M_{h}}{zQ}\left(\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}f_{1}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp}+\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}h_{1}^{\perp}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\right)\right]\biggr]\biggl\}\,,

\displaystyle W_{LU}^{\sin\phi_{h}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\biggl\{\Im\biggl[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}-\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\biggl[\frac{2xM_{N}}{Q}\left(\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp}D_{1}+\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}\,H_{1}^{\perp}\right)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}+\frac{2M_{h}}{zQ}\left(\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}f_{1}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp}+\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}h_{1}^{\perp}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\right)\biggr]}\biggr]\biggr\}\,.

(5.42)

Longitudinally polarized structure functions.

\displaystyle W_{UL}^{\sin\phi_{h}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{q_{T}}{Q}\frac{2k_{Tx}p_{Tx}-{\vec{k}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}{M_{N}M_{h}}\,\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,h_{1L}^{\perp}H_{1}^{\perp}}~+~{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\frac{k_{T}^{2}p_{Tx}+k_{Tx}p_{T}^{2}}{M_{N}M_{h}Q}h_{1L}^{\perp}H_{1}^{\perp}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Re\biggl[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\biggl[\frac{2xM_{N}}{Q}\left(\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp}_{L}D_{1}-\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{L}H_{1}^{\perp}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}-\frac{2M_{h}}{zQ}\left(-\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}\mathrm{i}g_{1L}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp}+\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}h_{1L}^{\perp}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\right)\biggr]}\biggr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\biggl\{\frac{2k_{T}^{2}p_{Tx}}{M_{N}M_{h}Q}\,\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\,h_{1L}^{\perp}H_{1}^{\perp}+\Re\biggl[\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\biggl[\frac{2xM_{N}}{Q}\left(\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp}_{L}D_{1}-\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{L}H_{1}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{2M_{h}}{zQ}\left(-\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}\mathrm{i}g_{1L}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp}+\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}h_{1L}^{\perp}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\right)\biggr]\biggr]\biggr\}\,,

(5.43)

\displaystyle W_{LL}^{\cos\phi_{h}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-\frac{q_{T}}{Q}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}g_{1L}D_{1}}-{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\frac{k_{Tx}+p_{Tx}}{Q}g_{1L}D_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}-\Im\biggl[\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\left[\frac{2xM_{N}}{Q}\left(\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp}_{L}D_{1}-\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{L}H_{1}^{\perp}\right)-\frac{2M_{h}}{zQ}\left(-\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}\mathrm{i}g_{1L}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp}+\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}h_{1L}^{\perp}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\right)\right]}\biggr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\biggl\{-\frac{2k_{Tx}}{Q}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}g_{1L}D_{1}-\Im\biggl[\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\biggl[\frac{2xM_{N}}{Q}\left(\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}^{\perp}_{L}D_{1}-\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{L}H_{1}^{\perp}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2M_{h}}{zQ}\left(-\frac{p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}\mathrm{i}g_{1L}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp}+\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}h_{1L}^{\perp}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}\right)\biggr]\biggr]\biggr\}\,.

(5.44)

Transversely polarized structure functions.

\displaystyle W_{UT}^{\sin\phi_{S}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\biggl\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}-\frac{q_{T}}{2Q}\,\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\left(\frac{k_{Tx}}{M_{N}}f_{1T}^{\perp}D_{1}-\frac{2p_{Tx}}{M_{h}}h_{1}H_{1}^{\perp}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad+{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.5}\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\left(-\frac{k_{T}^{2}+{\vec{k}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}{2M_{N}Q}f_{1T}^{\perp}D_{1}+\frac{p_{T}^{2}+{\vec{k}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}{M_{h}Q}h_{1}H_{1}^{\perp}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\Re\biggl[{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\mathcal{H}^{(1)}\biggl[\frac{xM_{N}}{Q}\biggl(2\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-7.0mu\tilde{f}_{T}D_{1}-\frac{{\vec{k}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}{M_{N}M_{h}}\Bigl(\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}-\underline{\hbox{}}\mkern-8.0mu\tilde{h}_{T}^{\perp}\Bigr)H_{1}^{\perp}\biggr)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}-\frac{M_{h}}{zQ}\biggl(2h_{1}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{H}+\frac{{\vec{k}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}{M_{N}M_{h}}\left(-\mathrm{i}g_{1T}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp}+f_{1T}^{\perp}\underline{\hbox{~}}\mkern-11.0mu\tilde{D}^{\perp}\right)\biggr)\biggr]}\biggr]\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathcal{F}}\biggl\{\mathcal{H}^{(0)}\biggl[-\frac{k_{T}^{2}}{M_{N}Q}f_{1T}^{\perp}D_{1}+\frac{2{\vec{k}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}{M_{h}Q}h_{1}H_{1}^{\perp}\biggr]
