, which give the proper Wilson lines for the fragmentation matrix element and together combine to
T_{n}(b_{\perp},0_{\perp})
for the staple needed in the TMD PDF and the closed Wilson loop needed for the soft function,

\displaystyle Z_{n}(b_{\perp})Z_{n}^{\dagger}(0_{\perp})

\displaystyle=T_{n}(b_{\perp},0_{\perp})\,,

\displaystyle U_{n}(b_{\perp})U_{n}^{\dagger}(0_{\perp})

\displaystyle=T_{n}^{s}(b_{\perp},0_{\perp})\,.

(C.11)

The analysis in ref.
[
29
]
corresponds to the choice of
\infty n^{\mu}+\infty a_{\perp}^{\mu}
as the reference point for these steps, and it was argued there that the choice of
a_{\perp}^{\mu}
did not matter.

We now consider the generalization of this discussion of transverse Wilson lines to the operators that give non-zero contributions at NLP.
Generalizing the LP analysis of transverse lines to the
\mathcal{B}_{n_{i}\perp}
operators given in eq. (
4.77
) is fairly straightforward. Each of the three
\omega_{i}
momenta appearing in this operator can be written as Fourier transforms of fields at light-cone positions
b_{i}^{+}
in position space. The quark and gluon fields that are next to each other and in the same
n_{i}
collinear direction, get connected by a finite length Wilson line from (for eg.)
b_{1}^{+}
to
b_{3}^{+}
. Thus the only transverse lines that are needed, occur in the parts of the operator that are next to the
S^{\dagger}_{{\bar{n}}_{1}}S_{n_{1}}
, just like in the LP operator in eq. (
C.10
). For this reason, the exact same type of modifications end up holding for the NLP quark-gluon-quark correlators, giving

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}\,f/N}^{\rho\beta\beta^{\prime}}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\theta(\omega_{a})\Bigl\langle N\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{n}^{\beta^{\prime}}(b_{\perp})\,T_{n}(b_{\perp},0_{\perp})\,g{\mathcal{B}}_{n\perp,\,-\xi\omega_{a}}^{\rho}(0)\,\chi_{n,\,(1-\xi)\omega_{a}}^{\beta}(0)\Bigr\rvert N\Bigr\rangle\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}^{\rho\,\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2zN_{c}}\theta(\omega_{b})\SumInt_{X_{\bar{n}}}\operatorname{tr}\Bigl\langle 0\Bigr\rvert Z_{\bar{n}}^{\dagger}(b_{\perp})\chi_{\bar{n}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(b_{\perp})\Bigr\rvert h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Bigl\langle h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{\bar{n},\,-(1-\xi)\omega_{b}}^{\alpha}(0)\,g{\mathcal{B}}_{\bar{n}\perp,\,\xi\omega_{b}}^{\rho}(0)Z_{\bar{n}}(0_{\perp})\Bigr\rvert 0\Bigr\rangle\,.

(C.20)

For the case of
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
operator contribution, we note that
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
must act on a gauge invariant object, so (suppressing Dirac matrices) we can modify the
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
operators as

\bar{\chi}_{n_{2}}[S^{\dagger}_{n_{2}}S_{n_{1}}]\,\mathcal{P}_{n_{1}\perp}\,\chi_{n_{1}}\to\bar{\chi}_{n_{2}}Z_{n_{2}}[U^{\dagger}_{n_{2}}S^{\dagger}_{n_{2}}S_{n_{1}}U_{n_{1}}]\,\mathcal{P}_{n_{1}\perp}\,Z_{n_{1}}^{\dagger}\chi_{n_{1}}\,,

(C.21)

with an analogous result for the term involving
\mathcal{P}_{n_{2}\perp}^{\dagger}
.
Then, the modified
\hat{B}_{\mathcal{P}\,f/N}
becomes

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{\mathcal{P}f/N}^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle\equiv\,\frac{\theta({\omega_{a}})}{2Q}\biggl\{\Bigl\langle N\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{n}^{\beta^{\prime}}(b_{\perp}^{\mu})Z_{n}(b_{\perp})\,\left[\not{\mathcal{P}}_{\perp}\not{{\bar{n}}}\,Z_{n}^{\dagger}(0_{\perp})\chi_{n,\omega_{a}}(0)\right]^{\beta}\Bigr\rvert N\Bigr\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\Bigl\langle N\Bigr\rvert\left[\bar{\chi}_{n}(b_{\perp}^{\mu})\,Z_{n}(b_{\perp})\not{{\bar{n}}}\,\not{\mathcal{P}}_{\perp}^{\dagger}\right]^{\beta^{\prime}}\,Z_{n}^{\dagger}(0_{\perp})\chi_{n,\omega_{a}}^{\beta}(0)\Bigr\rvert N\Bigr\rangle\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\,\frac{-\mathrm{i}}{2Q}\frac{\partial}{\partial b_{\perp}^{\rho}}\left[\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}\,\not{{\bar{n}}}\,,\,\hat{B}_{f/N}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})\right]^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}\,.

(C.22)

The second equality holds since
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
acts like a derivative to the 2-dimensional transverse local points at
0_{\perp}
or
b_{\perp}
. After rewriting
\mathcal{P}_{\perp}
as a derivative, it can be pulled out, and then
Z_{n}(b_{\perp})
and
Z_{n}(0_{\perp})
combine to give the
T_{n}(b_{\perp},0_{\perp})
that appears in
\hat{B}_{f/N}
, so it is the
\hat{B}_{f/N}
with the transverse link that appears on the last line of eq. (
C
). Thus we find that relation between NLP and LP TMD PDFs in eq. (
4.225b
) is still valid in the presence of transverse Wilson lines. A similar story also applies to
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{P}h/f}
with transverse lines, and its relation to
\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{h/f}
with transverse lines,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{P}\,h/f}^{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{\theta(\omega_{b})}{4zN_{c}Q}\SumInt_{X_{\bar{n}}}\biggl\{\Bigl\langle 0\Bigr\rvert\left[\not{\mathcal{P}}_{\perp}\not{n}\,Z_{\bar{n}}^{\dagger}(b_{\perp})\chi_{\bar{n}}(b_{\perp})\right]^{\alpha^{\prime}}\Bigr\rvert h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rangle\Bigl\langle h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{\bar{n},-\omega_{b}}^{\alpha}(0)Z_{\bar{n}}(0_{\perp})\Bigr\rvert 0\Bigr\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\Bigl\langle 0\Bigr\rvert Z_{\bar{n}}(b_{\perp})\chi_{\bar{n}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(b_{\perp})\Bigr\rvert h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rangle\Bigl\langle h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rvert\left[\bar{\chi}_{\bar{n},-\omega_{b}}(0)\,Z_{\bar{n}}^{\dagger}(0_{\perp})\not{n}\,\not{\mathcal{P}}_{\perp}^{\dagger}\right]^{\alpha}\Bigr\rvert 0\Bigr\rangle\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2Q}\frac{\partial}{\partial b_{\perp}^{\rho}}\left[\gamma_{\perp}^{\rho}\,\not{n}\,,\,\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{h/f}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})\right]^{\alpha^{\prime}\alpha}\,.

(C.31)

Hence we find that eq. (
4.226j
) also remains valid including transverse Wilson lines.

In conclusion, all the results presented in the main text remain valid with the inclusion of transverse Wilson lines as presented by the explicit formulas given here.

Appendix D
Relation to Correlators in the Literature

Our results heavily make use of the decompositions of quark-quark and quark-gluon-quark correlators
in the literature, which is required to make a connection to our results for the structure functions.
In our work, we use different conventions for the lightcone notation, and different definitions
of our correlators. In this appendix, we show how to relate our correlators to those in the literature,
and use this to obtain decompositions of our correlators since the general Lorentz structure of these correlators is identical to the ones that have been considered in the past. All our reference results for these decompositions are taken
from ref.
[
21
]
, up to using certain combinations of the functions therein,
which we will point out explicitly.

D.1
Lightcone Conventions

Our conventions are based on two reference vectors
n^{\mu}
and
{\bar{n}}^{\mu}

with
n^{2}={\bar{n}}^{2}=0
and
n\cdot{\bar{n}}=2
, and we define the lightcone coordinates
as
p^{-}={\bar{n}}\cdot p
and
p^{+}=n\cdot p
, see
section
2.3
.
Ref.
[
21
]
uses reference vectors
n_{\pm}^{\mu}
with
n_{+}^{2}=n_{-}^{2}=0

and
n_{+}\cdot n_{-}=1
, and defines lightcone components as
\tilde{p}^{\pm}=n_{\mp}\cdot p
.
To distinguish the two conventions, in this section we will always denote their lightcone
coordinates using a tilde. The two conventions are related by

\displaystyle n_{+}^{\mu}=\frac{n^{\mu}}{\sqrt{2}}\,,\quad n_{-}^{\mu}=\frac{{\bar{n}}^{\mu}}{\sqrt{2}}\,,\quad\tilde{p}^{\pm}=\frac{p^{\mp}}{\sqrt{2}}\,.

(D.1)

The first two relations are fixed by having the incoming momentum
P^{\mu}
be aligned
along the
n
and
n_{+}
direction, respectively.

D.2
Quark-Quark Correlator

The quark-quark correlator in ref.
[
21
]

is defined in their Eq. (3.10) as

\displaystyle\Phi_{ij}(x,{\vec{p}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}\tilde{\xi}^{-}\mathrm{d}^{2}\vec{\xi}_{T}}{(2\pi)^{3}}e^{\mathrm{i}x\tilde{P}^{+}\tilde{\xi}^{-}}e^{-\mathrm{i}{\vec{p}}_{T}\cdot\vec{\xi}_{T}}\braket{N|\bar{\psi}_{j}(0)\,\mathcal{U}^{n_{-}}_{(0,\infty)}\,\mathcal{U}^{n_{-}}_{(\infty,\xi)}\,\psi_{i}(\xi)|N}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}\xi^{+}\mathrm{d}^{2}\vec{\xi}_{T}}{\sqrt{2}(2\pi)^{3}}e^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}xP^{-}\xi^{+}}e^{-\mathrm{i}{\vec{p}}_{T}\cdot\vec{\xi}_{T}}\braket{N|\bar{\psi}_{j}(0)\mathcal{U}^{n_{-}}_{(0,\infty)}\,\mathcal{U}^{n_{-}}_{(\infty,\xi)}\psi_{i}(\xi)|N}\,,

(D.2)

where
ij
are spinor indices, and the quark flavor is kept implicit.
The Wilson lines
\mathcal{U}
will be converted to our
W_{n}
Wilson lines below.
In the first line, we use their lightcone convention, while in the second line we convert to our notation.
Next, we use that the correlator only depends on the relative position
0-\xi=-\xi

to shift the positions of the fields. Defining
b\equiv-\xi
, this yields
27
27

27

In recent work by Collins
[
27
]
,
the fields are evaluated as
\bar{\psi}(w/2)W(w/2,-w/2)\psi(-w/2)
,
which by the same argument is identical to our convention.

\displaystyle\Phi_{ij}(x,{\vec{p}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b^{+}\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{b}}_{T}}{\sqrt{2}(2\pi)^{3}}e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}xP^{-}b^{+}}e^{\mathrm{i}{\vec{p}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{b}}_{T}}\braket{N|\bar{\psi}_{j}(b)\mathcal{U}^{n_{-}}_{(b,\infty)}\,\mathcal{U}^{n_{-}}_{(\infty,0)}\psi_{i}(0)|N}\,.

(D.3)

This ensures that the Fourier phase for the transverse integration has the same sign as in our convention.
This yields the position-space correlator

\displaystyle\Phi_{ij}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{d}^{2}{\vec{p}}_{T}\,e^{-\mathrm{i}{\vec{b}}_{T}\cdot{\vec{p}}_{T}}\Phi_{ij}(x,{\vec{p}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b^{+}}{2\pi}e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}xP^{-}b^{+}}\braket{N|\bar{\psi}_{j}(b)\mathcal{U}^{n_{-}}_{(b,\infty)}\,\mathcal{U}^{n_{-}}_{(\infty,0)}\psi_{i}(0)|N}\,.

(D.4)

It remains to rewrite their Wilson lines
\mathcal{U}^{n_{-}}
in terms of our
W_{n}
.
The relation is

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{n_{-}}_{(\infty,0)}

\displaystyle=P\exp\left[-\mathrm{i}g\int_{\infty}^{0}\mathrm{d}s\,{\bar{n}}\cdot A(s{\bar{n}})\right]

\displaystyle=W_{n}^{\dagger}(0)\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{n_{-}}_{(b,\infty)}

\displaystyle=P\exp\left[-\mathrm{i}g\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}s\,{\bar{n}}\cdot A(s{\bar{n}}+b)\right]

\displaystyle=W_{n}(b)\,,

(D.5)

where we note that the
P
s written here would be
\bar{P}
s in our notation, and we neglected transverse Wilson lines for brevity.
The first equality is the definition of ref.
[
21
]
,
converted to our conventions, and in the second step we used eq. (
3.6
).
Inserting eq. (
D.2
) into eq. (
D.2
) yields

\displaystyle\Phi_{ij}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b^{+}}{2\pi}e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}xP^{-}b^{+}}\braket{N|\bar{\psi}_{j}(b)W_{n}(b)W_{n}^{\dagger}(0)\psi_{i}(0)|N}\,,

(D.6)

where we remind the reader that
b^{\mu}=(b^{+},0,b_{\perp})
.

Our beam function

is defined in eq. (
4.2
) as

\displaystyle\hat{B}^{ij}(x=\omega_{a}/P_{N}^{-},{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\braket{N|\bar{\chi}_{n}^{j}(b_{\perp})\chi_{n,\omega_{a}}^{i}(0)|N}\,,

(D.7)

where here we label the spinor indices as
ij
instead of
\beta\beta^{\prime}
.
For consistency with eq. (
D.2
), we also and suppress the flavor and hadron labels,
as well as the explicit
\theta(\omega_{a})
factor present in eq. (
4.2
).
Inserting the definitions of the collinear quark fields from eq. (
3.3
),

\displaystyle\chi_{n,\omega}(x)

\displaystyle=\Bigl[\delta(\omega-\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{n})\,W_{n}^{\dagger}(x)\,\xi_{n}(x)\Bigr]\,,

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}_{n,\omega}(x)

\displaystyle=(\chi_{n,-\omega})^{\dagger}\gamma^{0}=\Bigl[\bar{\xi}_{n}(x)W_{n}(x)\delta(\omega+\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{n}^{\dagger})\Bigr]\,,

(D.8)

the beam correlator becomes

\displaystyle\hat{B}^{ij}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\Braket{N|\bar{\xi}_{n}^{j}(b_{\perp})W_{n}(b_{\perp})\bigl[\delta(\omega_{a}-\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{n})\,W_{n}^{\dagger}(0)\,\xi_{n}^{i}(0)\bigr]|N}\,.

(D.9)

The combination in square brackets has a fixed longitudinal momentum
\omega_{a}
,
which can equivalently be expressed through the Fourier-transformed field

\displaystyle\bigl[\delta(\omega_{a}-\overline{\mathcal{P}}_{n})\,W_{n}^{\dagger}(0)\,\xi_{n}^{i}(0)\bigr]

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b^{+}}{4\pi}e^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\omega_{a}(-b^{+})}W_{n}^{\dagger}(-b^{+})\,\xi_{n}^{i}(-b^{+})\,.

(D.10)

This puts the
\xi_{n}
field in eq. (
D.9
) to position
-b^{+}

which we again shift into the first
\bar{\xi}_{n}
field,

\displaystyle\hat{B}^{ij}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b^{+}}{4\pi}e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\omega_{a}b^{+}}\Braket{N|\bar{\xi}_{n}^{j}(b)W_{n}(b)W_{n}^{\dagger}(0)\,\xi_{n}^{i}(0)|N}\,,

(D.11)

where now
b^{\mu}=(b^{+},0,b_{\perp})
.
The
n
-collinear fields
\xi_{n}
only consistent of the “good components” of the quark field,
which are projected out as

\displaystyle\xi_{n}^{i}=\frac{(\not{n}\not{{\bar{n}}})^{ii^{\prime}}}{4}\psi_{i^{\prime}}\,,\qquad\bar{\xi}_{n}^{j}=\bar{\psi}_{j^{\prime}}\frac{(\not{{\bar{n}}}\not{n})^{j^{\prime}j}}{4}\,,

(D.12)

where we make the spinor indices explicit. Inserting this into eq. (
D.11
) yields

\displaystyle\hat{B}^{ij}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{(\not{n}\not{{\bar{n}}})^{ii^{\prime}}}{4}\frac{(\not{{\bar{n}}}\not{n})^{jj^{\prime}}}{4}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b^{+}}{4\pi}e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\omega_{a}b^{+}}\Braket{N|\bar{\psi}_{j^{\prime}}(b)W_{n}(b)W_{n}^{\dagger}(0)\,\psi_{i^{\prime}}(0)|N}\,.

(D.13)

Comparing eqs. (
D.2
) and (
D.13
), we finally obtain the desired relation

\displaystyle\hat{B}^{ij}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{(\not{n}\not{{\bar{n}}})^{ii^{\prime}}}{4}\,\frac{\Phi_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})}{\sqrt{2}}\,\frac{(\not{{\bar{n}}}\not{n})^{j^{\prime}j}}{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}\bigl[\not{n}_{+}\not{n}_{-}\Phi(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})\not{n}_{-}\not{n}_{+}\bigr]^{ij}\,.

(D.14)

In the second line, we have made use of eq. (
D.1
).
Since the projection operators and constant factors commute
with taking the Fourier transform, one obtains the same
result for the Fourier-transformed correlators.

Decomposition.

Eq. (
D.2
) allows us to easily obtain the spin-dependendent
decomposition of
\hat{B}^{ij}
from that of
\Phi^{ij}
given in
Eq. (3.15) of ref.
[
21
]
,

\displaystyle\Phi(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\biggl(f_{1}-f_{1T}^{\perp}\frac{\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}k_{\perp\rho}S_{\perp\sigma}}{M}+g_{1s}\gamma_{5}+h_{1T}\gamma_{5}\not{S}_{\perp}+h_{1s}^{\perp}\gamma^{5}\frac{\not{k}_{\perp}}{M}+\mathrm{i}h_{1}^{\perp}\frac{\not{k}_{\perp}}{M}\biggr)\frac{\not{n}_{+}}{2}

\displaystyle\quad+(\mathrm{higher~twist})\,,

(D.15)

where all functions on the right-hand side are evaluated as
f_{i}\equiv f_{i}(x,k_{T})
.
Inserting this into eq. (
D.2
) only requires to use

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2}}(\not{n}_{+}\not{n}_{-})\frac{\not{n}_{+}}{2}(\not{n}_{-}\not{n}_{+})=\frac{\not{n}_{+}}{2\sqrt{2}}=\frac{\not{n}}{4}\,.

(D.16)

We arrive at the desired result

\displaystyle\hat{B}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\biggl(f_{1}-f_{1T}^{\perp}\frac{\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}k_{\perp\rho}S_{\perp\sigma}}{M}+g_{1s}\gamma_{5}+h_{1T}\gamma_{5}\not{S}_{\perp}+h_{1s}^{\perp}\frac{\gamma^{5}\not{k}_{\perp}}{M}+\mathrm{i}h_{1}^{\perp}\frac{\not{k}_{\perp}}{M}\biggr)\frac{\not{n}}{4}\,.

(D.17)

Note that all higher-twist terms vanish because
\not{n}_{\pm}^{2}=0
,
and only the leading twist contribution has the
\not{n}_{+}
structure
preventing one from commuting a
\not{n}_{-}
past
\Phi
in eq. (
D.2
).
Since
h_{1s}^{\perp}
is multiplied by a term proportional to
\not{k}_{\perp}

in eq. (
D.17
), it contributes a linear and quadratic Lorentz structure.
It is useful to split off a traceless piece, as the tracelessness is preservered
by the Fourier transform we ultimately need to take. This leads to

\displaystyle B_{f/N}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\biggl[f_{1}-f_{1T}^{\perp}\frac{\epsilon_{\perp}^{\rho\sigma}k_{\perp\rho}S_{\perp\sigma}}{M_{N}}+g_{1L}\,S_{L}\gamma_{5}-g_{1T}\frac{k_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}}{M_{N}}\gamma_{5}+h_{1}\,\gamma_{5}\not{S}_{\perp}

\displaystyle\qquad+h_{1L}^{\perp}\,S_{L}\frac{\gamma_{5}\,\not{k}_{\perp}}{M_{N}}-h_{1T}^{\perp}\,\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{M_{N}^{2}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{2}g_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}-\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}k_{\perp}^{\nu}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\Bigr)S_{\perp\,\mu}\gamma_{\nu}\gamma_{5}+\mathrm{i}h_{1}^{\perp}\frac{\not{k}_{\perp}}{M_{N}}\biggr]\frac{\not{n}}{4}\,.

(D.18)

Here, we defined the correlator
[
21
]

\displaystyle h_{1}(x,k_{T})=h_{1T}(x,k_{T})-\frac{k_{\perp}^{2}}{2M_{N}^{2}}h_{1T}^{\perp}(x,k_{T})\,.

(D.19)

We have also replaced the shorthand notations
g_{1s}
and
h_{1s}
by
[
21
]

\displaystyle g_{1s}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})

\displaystyle=S_{L}\,g_{1L}(x,k_{T})-\frac{k_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}}{M_{N}}\,g_{1T}(x,k_{T})\,,

\displaystyle h_{1s}^{\perp}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})

\displaystyle=S_{L}\,h_{1L}^{\perp}(x,k_{T})-\frac{k_{\perp}\cdot S_{\perp}}{M_{N}}\,h_{1T}^{\perp}(x,k_{T})\,.

(D.20)

D.3
Quark-Gluon-Quark Correlator

Here, we repeat the steps shown in
appendix
D.2
for the quark-gluon-quark correlators.
Most intermediate steps have been discussed in full detail in there,
and here we will limit ourselves to the key steps.

D.3.1
TMDPDF

We begin with the quark-gluon-quark correlator for the incoming hadron,
i.e. corresponding to the TMDPDF. In ref.
[
21
]
, it is defined as

\displaystyle(\tilde{\Phi}_{A}^{\rho})^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b^{+}}{\sqrt{2}(2\pi)}e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}xP_{N}^{-}b^{+}}\braket{h(P)|\bar{\psi}^{\beta^{\prime}}(b)gA_{T}^{\rho}(0)\psi^{\beta}(0)|h(P)}\,,

(D.21)

where as usual
b=(b^{+},0,b_{\perp})
.
Here, we have already converted to our conventions following the same steps as in
appendix
D.2
.
Note that
A_{T}^{\rho}
is the transverse part of the gluon field, and hence
\rho
is a transverse index.
For simplicity, we use light-cone gauge with
A^{+}=0
but neglect transverse Wilson lines.
Note that ref.
[
21
]
also defines a correlator
(\Phi_{A}^{\rho})^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}

similar to eq. (
D.21
) but with a covariant derivative
\mathrm{i}D^{\mu}
instead of the gluon field.
Since its large component can be reduced to the leading-power correlator,
it is subtracted out to yield the purely transverse correlator in eq. (
D.21
).
In our approach, this is built in by using a collinear gluon field from the beginning.

We contrast this with our correlator as defined in eq. (
4.7
),

\displaystyle\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\rho\beta\beta^{\prime}}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\Bigl\langle N\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{n}^{\beta^{\prime}}(b_{\perp})\,g{\mathcal{B}}_{n\perp,\,-\xi\omega_{a}}^{\rho}(0)\,\chi_{n,\,(1-\xi)\omega_{a}}^{\beta}(0)\Bigr\rvert N\Bigr\rangle

(D.22)

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b_{1}^{+}}{4\pi}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b_{2}^{+}}{4\pi}e^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}[\xi b_{1}^{+}+(1-\xi)b_{2}^{+}]xP_{N}^{-}}\Bigl\langle N\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{n}^{\beta^{\prime}}(b_{\perp})\,g{\mathcal{B}}_{n\perp}^{\rho}(-b_{1}^{+})\,\chi_{n}^{\beta}(-b_{2}^{+})\Bigr\rvert N\Bigr\rangle\,.

Here, we suppressed the overall
\theta(\omega_{a})
for simplicity,
and in the second step we replaced the fields with fixed momentum fraction
by their Fourier transform as in eq. (
D.10
).
Note that only the
\bar{\chi}_{n}
field has a transverse position,
while the other fields only have a longitudinal displacement.
In particular, the fields have distinct lightcone positions,
reflecting that one is sensitive to their individual momenta.

To relate eq. (
D.22
) to eq. (
D.21
),
we integrate over
\xi
which fixes
b_{1}^{+}=b_{2}^{+}\equiv-b^{+}
.
Shifting this position into the antiquark field, we obtain

\displaystyle\int\!\mathrm{d}\xi\,\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\rho\beta\beta^{\prime}}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})=\frac{1}{xP_{N}^{-}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b^{+}}{4\pi}e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}b_{2}^{+}xP_{N}^{-}}\Bigl\langle N\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{n}^{\beta^{\prime}}(b)\,g{\mathcal{B}}_{n\perp}^{\rho}(0)\chi_{n}^{\beta}(0)\Bigr\rvert N\Bigr\rangle\,,

(D.23)

where now
b^{\mu}=(b^{+},0,b_{\perp})
.
By replacing the good components
\chi_{n}
of the collinear quarks with the projection
onto the full collinear fields as in eq. (
D.12
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\int\!\mathrm{d}\xi\,\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\rho\beta\beta^{\prime}}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})=\frac{(\not{n}\not{{\bar{n}}})^{\beta\alpha}}{4}\frac{(\tilde{\Phi}_{A}^{\rho})^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}(x,{\vec{b}}_{T})}{\sqrt{2}xP_{N}^{-}}\frac{(\not{{\bar{n}}}\not{n})^{\alpha^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}}{4}\,.

(D.24)

We remark that here, we have neglected some Wilson lines, which were dropped in writing eq. (
D.21
).
In eq. (
D.23
), collinear quark and gluon fields contain Wilson lines
to yield gauge-invariant building blocks. Likewise, for a general gauge ref.
[
21
]

provides a gauge-invariant definition of eq. (
D.21
)
that dresses the fields in there with Wilson lines.
Since anyway our Wilson line structure will differ, due to the appearance of the extra momentum fraction
\xi
, we do not bother to demonstrate explicitly that upon integration over
\xi
the Wilson line structures match between the approaches.
Both procedures should anyway be unique, up to choosing the Wilson line directions as outgoing or incoming
as appropriate for SIDIS or Drell-Yan kinematics.

Finally, we remark that the projectors in eq. (
D.24
) have no practical
effect, as the correlators
\tilde{\Phi}_{A}
correlators in ref.
[
21
]

are parameterized in momentum space without writing out subleading power terms, with the form

\displaystyle\tilde{\Phi}_{A}^{\rho}(x,{\vec{k}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{xM_{N}}{2}\bigl\{\cdots\bigr\}\frac{\not{n}_{+}}{2}\,,

(D.25)

where the ellipses contain the individual TMDs and will be presented in eq. (
5.2.3
).
Inserting this into eq. (
D.24
) and using that
n_{+}=n/\sqrt{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int\!\mathrm{d}\xi\,\hat{B}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\rho\beta\beta^{\prime}}(x,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{N}}{4P_{N}^{-}}\bigl\{\cdots\bigr\}\frac{\not{n}}{2}\,.

(D.26)

D.3.2
TMDFF

Next, we consider the quark-gluon-quark correlator for the fragmenting hadron,
i.e. corresponding to the TMDPDF. In ref.
[
21
]
, it is defined as
28
28

28

Note that the color average is not made explicit in ref.
[
21
]
.
Following e.g. ref.
[
27
]
, we have reinstated it here.

\displaystyle(\tilde{\Delta}_{A}^{\rho})^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2zN_{c}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b^{-}}{\sqrt{2}(2\pi)}e^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}b^{-}(P_{h}^{+}/z)}\SumInt_{X}\braket{0|gA_{T}^{\rho}(b)\psi_{\alpha}(b)|hX}\braket{hX|\bar{\psi}_{\alpha^{\prime}}(0)|0}\,,

where
b=(0,b^{-},b_{\perp})
, and as before we use lightcone gauge for simplicity.
We will also require the following conjugate operator:

\displaystyle\bigl[\gamma_{0}\tilde{\Delta}_{A}^{\dagger\rho}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})\gamma_{0}\bigr]^{\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2zN_{c}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b^{-}}{\sqrt{2}(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}b^{-}(P_{h}^{+}/z)}

\displaystyle\quad\times\SumInt_{X}\braket{0|\psi_{\alpha^{\prime}}(0)|hX}\braket{hX|gA_{T}^{\rho}(b)\bar{\psi}_{\alpha}(b)|0}\,.

Our corresponding correlator is defined in eq. (
4.7
) as

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}^{\rho\,\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2zN_{c}}\SumInt_{X_{\bar{n}}}\operatorname{tr}\Bigl\langle 0\Bigr\rvert\chi_{\bar{n}}^{\alpha}(b_{\perp})\Bigr\rvert h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rangle\Bigl\langle h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{\bar{n},\,-(1-\xi)\omega_{b}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(0)\,g{\mathcal{B}}_{\bar{n}\perp,\,\xi\omega_{b}}^{\rho}(0)\Bigr\rvert 0\Bigr\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2zN_{c}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b_{1}^{-}}{4\pi}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b_{2}^{-}}{4\pi}e^{\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}[(1-\xi)b_{1}^{-}+\xi b_{2}^{-}](P_{h}^{+}/z)}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\SumInt_{X_{\bar{n}}}\operatorname{tr}\Bigl\langle 0\Bigr\rvert\chi_{\bar{n}}^{\alpha}(b_{\perp})\Bigr\rvert h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rangle\Bigl\langle h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{\bar{n}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(b_{1}^{-})\,g{\mathcal{B}}_{\bar{n}\perp}^{\rho}(b_{2}^{-})\Bigr\rvert 0\Bigr\rangle\,.

Here, we suppress all
\theta
functions for brevity, and in the second step
replaced the fields of fixed label momentum by their Fourier representation as in eq. (
D.10
).

To relate eq. (
D.3.2
) to eq. (
D.3.2
),
we integrate over
\xi
which fixes
b_{1}^{-}=b_{2}^{-}\equiv-b^{-}
,
and we shift the field positions by
b^{-}
. This yields

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}\xi\,\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}^{\rho\,\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2zN_{c}}\frac{1}{P_{h}^{+}/z}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}b^{-}}{4\pi}e^{-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}b^{-}(P_{h}^{+}/z)}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\SumInt_{X_{\bar{n}}}\operatorname{tr}\Bigl\langle 0\Bigr\rvert\chi_{\bar{n}}^{\alpha}(b)\Bigr\rvert h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rangle\Bigl\langle h,X_{\bar{n}}\Bigr\rvert\bar{\chi}_{\bar{n}}^{\alpha^{\prime}}(0)\,g{\mathcal{B}}_{\bar{n}\perp}^{\rho}(0)\Bigr\rvert 0\Bigr\rangle\,,

where now
b=(0,b^{-},b_{\perp})
.
Comparing this to eq. (
D.3.2
) by using the quark projection in eq. (
D.12
), we find that

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}\xi\,\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}^{\rho\,\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{(\not{{\bar{n}}}\not{n})^{\alpha\beta}}{4}\frac{\bigl[\gamma_{0}\tilde{\Delta}_{A}^{\dagger\rho}(z,{\vec{b}}_{T})\gamma_{0}\bigr]^{\beta\beta^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{2}P_{h}^{+}/z}\frac{(\not{n}\not{{\bar{n}}})^{\beta^{\prime}\alpha^{\prime}}}{4}\,.

(D.67)

This result is consistent with eq. (
D.24
) for the
B_{\mathcal{B}}
operator.
As in that case, here we do not explicitly verify the Wilson structures
in eq. (
D.3.2
) and the correlator in ref.
[
21
]

for a generic gauge.

Finally, we remark that the projectors in eq. (
D.67
) have no practical
effect, as the correlators
\tilde{\Delta}_{A}
correlators in ref.
[
21
]

are parameterized in momentum space in a form that does not contain power suppressed terms,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Delta}_{A}^{\rho}(z,{\vec{k}}_{T})=\frac{M_{h}}{2z}\bigl\{\cdots\bigr\}\frac{\not{n}_{-}}{2}\,,

(D.68)

where the ellipses contain the individual TMDs and will be presented in eq. (
5.26
).
Inserting this into eq. (
D.67
) and using that
n_{-}={\bar{n}}/\sqrt{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}\xi\,\hat{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathcal{B}\,h/f}^{\rho\,\alpha\alpha^{\prime}}(z,\xi,{\vec{b}}_{T})

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{h}}{4P_{h}^{+}}\bigl\{\cdots\bigr\}^{\dagger}\frac{\not{{\bar{n}}}}{2}\,.

(D.69)

Here, care has to be taken because
\bigl\{\cdots\bigr\}
contains imaginary parts
and needs to be conjugated, as indicated.
